why—and when the other options make sense.\n\n## Option 1: Kaohsiung MRT (Metro) – The Best Choice for\n\n## Most Travelers\n\n![kaohsuing-3](https://storage.googleapis.com/lk_blog_prod/images/winston-chen-4SD7fsm4NRQ-unsplash.original.jpg)\n\n### Why the Kaohsiung MRT is Incredibly Easy\n\nTaiwan's public transportation is world-class, and Kaohsiung's MRT system is clean, modern, safe, efficient, and ridiculously easy to navigate even if you've never been to Asia before. The airport sits directly on the **Red Line (R4 Station)**, which runs north-south through the entire city.\n\nYou don't need to leave the terminal building, navigate confusing exits, cross busy roads, or figure out bus stops. You literally walk from the arrivals hall directly into the MRT station underground—it's about a 2-minute walk with clear English signage the entire way.\n\n### How to Take the MRT from Kaohsiung Airport (Step-by-Step)\n\n**Step 1: Exit Customs and Collect Luggage** After passing through immigration and collecting your bags, you'll enter the arrivals hall.\n\n**Step 2: Optional Stops in Arrivals Hall**\n\n- **SIM Card**: If you need a Taiwan SIM card (highly recommended), telecom counters are on your left as you enter arrivals. Options include Chunghwa Telecom, Taiwan Mobile, and FarEasTone. Expect to pay NT$300-500 for 3-7 days of unlimited data.\n- **Currency Exchange**: If you need Taiwan dollars (NT$), there are exchange counters and ATMs in the arrivals hall. ATMs typically offer better rates than exchange counters.\n- **Tourist Information**: The tourist service center can provide maps and basic information.\n\n**Step 3: Follow MRT Signs** Look for clear signs saying **\"MRT\"** or **\"Metro\"** (with the red \"M\" logo). Turn left as you exit arrivals and walk toward the elevator/escalator area. You'll see signs pointing you downstairs.\n\n**Step 4: Take Elevator/Escalator Down** Head down one level to the MRT station. There are elevators if you have heavy luggage or accessibility needs.\n\n**Step 5: Purchase Ticket or Use Transit Card** You have two options:\n\n**Option A: Single-Journey Token** (for one-time use)\n\n- Approach the ticket vending machines\n- Select English language option (top right of screen)\n- Choose your destination station or select fare amount\n- Pay with cash (machines accept NT$10, NT$50, NT$100, NT$500 bills and coins)\n- Receive a plastic token (looks like a poker chip)\n- Keep this token—you'll need it to exit at your destination\n\n**Option B: EasyCard or iPASS Card** (HIGHLY RECOMMENDED)\n\n- Purchase at the airport MRT service counter or convenience store\n- EasyCard costs NT$100 (this is a deposit, refundable when you return the card)\n- Load credit onto the card (NT$100-500 to start)\n- Tap the card on readers to enter and exit—no need to buy individual tickets\n- Get automatic discounts on all public transport (about 20% off)\n- Can also be used on buses, ferries, YouBikes, and even at 7-Eleven and FamilyMart convenience stores throughout Taiwan\n\n**Pro tip**: If you're staying in Taiwan more than one day, absolutely get an EasyCard. It saves time, money, and eliminates the need to figure out fares every time you travel.\n\n**Step 6: Enter Through Turnstiles**\n\n- Insert your token into the slot (it will be retained temporarily) OR tap your EasyCard on the reader\n- Gates open automatically\n- Proceed to Platform 2 (northbound trains toward Gangshan South)\n\n**Step 7: Board the Red Line Train**\n\n- Trains arrive every **4-6 minutes during peak hours** (roughly 7am-9am and 5pm-8pm)\n- Every **6-8 minutes during off-peak times**\n- All trains heading north from the airport go to the city centre\n- Trains are clearly labeled in English\n- Digital displays show the next station\n- Announcements are in Mandarin and English\n\n**Step 8: Ride to Your Destination** Common stops and travel times from the airport:\n\n- **R6 Kaohsiung Arena (巨蛋站)**: 11 minutes\n- **R8 Sanduo Shopping District (三多商圈站)**: 13 minutes\n- **R9 Central Park (中央公園站)**: 14 minutes\n- **R10 Formosa Boulevard (美麗島站)**: 15 minutes\n- **R11 Kaohsiung Main Station (高雄車站)**: 17 minutes\n- **R14 Kaohsiung HSR Zuoying Station (左營站)**: 24 minutes\n\n**Step 9: Exit at Your Station**\n\n- Insert your token into the exit turnstile (it will be collected) OR tap your EasyCard\n- Follow signs to the exit number closest to your hotel/destination\n- Most stations have multiple exits—check which is closest to where you're going\n\n### MRT Operating Hours\n\n- **First train from airport**: Around 5:56am (weekdays and weekends)\n- **Last train from airport**: Around 12:02am (midnight)\n- If arriving on a late flight after midnight, peak commuter hours** (8-9:30am and 5-7pm weekdays) if possible—trains get crowded with office workers\n- All stations have elevators for accessibility\n- Escalators and stairs are also available\n\n### Transferring to the Orange or Green Line\n\nIf your hotel is on a different MRT line:\n\n**Orange Line Transfer**: Change at Formosa Boulevard Station (O5/R10)—the two lines cross here. Simply follow signs for the Orange Line platform.\n\n**Green Line (Light Rail) Transfer**: The circular Light Rail connects to the MRT at several stations. Most visitors don't need it for airport transfers.\n\n### Cost Breakdown\n\n- **Formosa Boulevard**: NT$30 (about US$1)\n- **Kaohsiung Main Station**: NT$35\n- **Zuoying Station (HSR)**: NT$40-50\n\n**Seriously—you can get from the airport to the city centre for one US dollar.** This is why the MRT is so popular.\n\n### Why We Love the Kaohsiung MRT\n\nBeyond being cheap and fast, the MRT offers:\n\n- **Air conditioning** (crucial in Taiwan's hot, humid weather)\n- **Cleanliness** (eating, drinking, and smoking are strictly prohibited)\n- **Safety** (Taiwan has extremely low crime rates)\n- **Free WiFi** at many stations\n- **English signage and announcements**\n- **Punctuality** (trains run exactly on schedule)\n\n**Bottom line**: If you're arriving during MRT operating hours (6am-midnight), have reasonable luggage, and want the quickest way into Kaohsiung, take the MRT. It's that simple.\n\n## Option 2: Taxi from Kaohsiung Airport to City Centre\n\n![Kaohsiung-4](https://storage.googleapis.com/lk_blog_prod/images/matt-tsai-XiuGDwRysak-unsplash.original.jpg)\n\nKaohsiung taxis are well-regulated, safe, and readily available outside both terminals.\n\n### Finding a Taxi at Kaohsiung Airport\n\n**Taxi Rank Location**: Directly outside the arrivals hall—you literally can't miss it. There's a clearly marked taxi queue with airport staff managing the line during busy periods.\n\n**Taxi Appearance**: Official airport taxis are yellow and have clear identification. Look for the taximeter inside.\n\n### What to Expect\n\n- **Drivers use meters**: This is legally required and strictly enforced at the airport\n- **Language barrier**: Many drivers speak limited or no English, though some drivers know basic phrases\n- **Cash preferred**: Most taxis accept cash only, though some newer vehicles have card payment options\n- **International terminal fee**: A legal NT$50 surcharge applies for pickups from the international terminal\n- **No need to tip**: Tipping is not expected in Taiwan\n\n### Kaohsiung Airport Taxi Costs\n\n- **To Formosa Boulevard/Liuhe Night Market area**: NT$250-300 (US$8-10)\n- **To Kaohsiung Main Station**: NT$280-320 (US$9-10)\n- **To Zuoying (HSR area)**: NT$350-400 (US$11-13)\n- **To Pier-2 Art Center area**: NT$300-350 (US$10-11)\n\n**Travel time**: 20-30 minutes depending on traffic and exact destination\n\n### When a Taxi Makes Sense\n\nTaxis are worth the extra cost if:\n\n- **You're arriving late at night** after MRT closes (after midnight)\n- **You have multiple large or heavy suitcases** and don't want to navigate MRT stairs\n- **You're traveling as a group of 3-4 people** (split cost makes it reasonable)\n- **Your hotel is not near an MRT station**\n- **You're exhausted from a long flight** and just want door-to-door convenience\n- **You have young children** and all the related gear\n\n### Communication Tips\n\n- **Have your destination written in Chinese**: Show the driver your hotel address in Chinese characters (your booking confirmation usually has this)\n- **Use Google Maps**: Open your destination in Google Maps and show the driver\n- **Translation apps**: Papago or Google Translate can help with basic communication\n\n### Safety and Quality\n\nTaiwan taxis are generally very honest. Scams are extremely rare, especially with official airport taxis. If you experience any issues:\n\n- Note the taxi number (displayed inside and outside the vehicle)\n- Taxi drivers fear complaints to airport authorities\n- Contact the airport complaint line if Uber from Kaohsiung Airport\n\n**Step 1**: Download the Uber app before your trip and set up payment\n\n**Step 2**: After collecting luggage, request a ride using the app\n\n**Step 3**: The pickup location is usually in the **parking lot across from the terminal building**—the app will show you exactly where\n\n**Step 4**: Walk outside the terminal, cross the street to the designated parking more for convenience and clarity\n- You want a record of your trip for expense reports\n\n## Option 4: Private Airport Transfer Service\n\nPre-booked private transfers offer maximum convenience at maximum cost.\n\n### What Is a Private Transfer?\n\nA dedicated driver waits for you at arrivals holding a sign with your name, helps with luggage, and drives you directly to your hotel door in a private vehicle.\n\n### How to Book\n\nBook through:\n\n- Your hotel (many offer transfer services)\n- Online platforms like Klook, KKday, or dedicated transfer companies\n- Travel agencies\n\nBook at least 2-3 days in advance, providing your flight details and hotel address.\n\n### Costs\n\n- **Airport to city centre**: NT$1000-1500 (US$32-48)\n- **Airport to Kenting**: NT$2500-3500 (US$80-110)\n- **Airport to Tainan**: NT$1800-2500 (US$58-80)\n\nPrices vary based on:\n\n- Vehicle size (sedan, van, luxury vehicle)\n- Time of day (night surcharges may apply)\n- Distance\n- Special requests (child seats, extra luggage space)\n\n### What's Included\n\n- Driver waiting at arrivals (even if flight is delayed)\n- Help with luggage\n- Door-to-door service\n- English-speaking driver (usually, but confirm when booking)\n- Fixed price (no meter, no surprises)\n\n### When Private Transfers Make Sense\n\n- **Families with young children**: Car seats can be arranged, no hassle with public transport\n- **Large groups (4+ people)**: Splitting the cost among several people makes it reasonable\n- **Elderly travelers** or those with mobility issues\n- **Very late or very early arrivals**: Peace of mind knowing someone will be waiting\n- **Business travelers**: Efficiency and comfort justify the cost\n- **Traveling beyond Kaohsiung**: If heading directly to Kenting, Tainan, or elsewhere, a private transfer can be more convenient than public transport\n\n## Option 5: Bus from Kaohsiung Airport\n\nBuses do operate from the airport, but they're **not the most practical Choose based on your interests rather than worrying about transportation—Kaohsiung's MRT makes everywhere accessible.\n\n## First-Time Tips for Arriving in Kaohsiung\n\n### Before You Land\n\n- **Download essential apps**: Google Maps (works perfectly in Taiwan), Google Translate or Papago (for Chinese translation)\n- **Know your hotel address in Chinese**: Screenshot it or save it offline\n- **Check MRT operating hours**: If arriving after midnight, plan for taxi/Uber\n- **Consider pre-booking a Taiwan SIM card**: Available online for airport pickup, or buy at airport counters\n\n### At the Airport\n\n- **Free WiFi is available**: Connect before leaving the terminal if you need to contact your hotel or check directions\n- **ATMs are readily available**: International cards work at most ATMs with no Kaohsiung airport-specific surcharges (your home bank may charge fees)\n- **Currency exchange**: Available but ATMs typically offer better rates\n- **English signage is everywhere**: Airport, MRT, and major stations all have clear English\n- **Taiwan is cash-based**: While cards work in hotels and larger restaurants, many small shops, night markets, and local eateries only accept cash\n\n### Cultural Notes\n\n- **Taiwan is incredibly safe**: Even at night, even for solo travelers\n- **People are helpful**: If you look lost, locals often approach to offer help (many young Taiwanese speak English)\n- **Cleanliness matters**: Taiwan is very clean; dispose of trash properly\n- **No tipping culture**: Tipping is not expected or practiced in Taiwan\n- **Public transport etiquette**: Queue orderly, let passengers exit before boarding, offer seats to elderly/pregnant people, no eating/drinking on MRT\n\n### Language Barrier Reality Check\n\n**Good news**: You can absolutely navigate Kaohsiung without knowing Mandarin.\n\n- MRT announcements are bilingual (Mandarin and English)\n- Station names displayed in English and Chinese\n- Many convenience store and restaurant workers speak basic English, especially younger people\n- Translation apps work well\n- Pointing, smiling, and Google Translate get you surprisingly far\n\nTaiwan ranks as one of the most foreigner-friendly destinations in Asia specifically because of clear English signage and helpful locals.\n\n## What to Do After You Arrive in Kaohsiung\n\n![Kaohsuing-5](https://storage.googleapis.com/lk_blog_prod/images/ricky-lk-QyhBZsQuBuc-unsplash.original.jpg)\n\nOnce you've checked into your accommodation, Kaohsiung really opens up—especially when you explore it with someone who actually lives here.\n\n### Beyond the Tourist Trail\n\nSure, you can hit the major attractions—Fo Guang Shan Monastery, Dragon and Tiger Pagodas, Love River, Liuhe Night Market. But Kaohsiung's real magic lives in its neighborhoods, local food scenes, and everyday rhythms that guidebooks barely mention.\n\nMany travelers prefer experiencing Kaohsiung through:\n\n- **Walking residential neighborhoods** with a local host who explains how daily life actually works\n- **Discovering food spots** they'd never find on their own—the breakfast stalls locals line up at, the family-run eateries serving authentic Taiwanese comfort food, the hidden dessert shops\n- **Learning cultural context** that transforms random observations into meaningful understanding\n- **Adjusting plans on the fly** based on what interests you most\n\n### Experience Kaohsiung With a Local Host\n\n![Kaohsiung-6](https://storage.googleapis.com/lk_blog_prod/images/vincent-chan-ncatIFCgB5c-unsplash.original.jpg)\n\nLanding in Kaohsiung is easy—the MRT gets you from airport to city centre in 15 minutes. But truly understanding this laid-back southern Taiwanese city takes local insight.\n\n**What's different about exploring with a local host?**\n\nInstead of following a rigid tour itinerary, you get:\n\n- A Kaohsiung local who adapts to your interests and energy level\n- Insider knowledge about where locals actually eat, shop, and spend time\n- Cultural explanations that make the city make sense\n- Help navigating Mandarin-only situations\n- Flexibility to linger where you're enjoying yourself and skip what doesn't resonate\n- Recommendations for the rest of your stay\n\nWhether you're in Kaohsiung for a day, a weekend, or longer, starting with a local-led experience helps you get oriented fast and discover sides of the city tourists typically miss.\n\n👉 [**Book a Private Walking Tour with a Kaohsiung Local Host**](https://lokafy.com/pricing) and explore the city beyond the guidebooks.\n\nLokafy connects you with friendly Kaohsiung locals who host personalized, flexible experiences tailored to what you actually want to see and do—not a one-size-fits-all tour script.\n\n## Getting from Kaohsiung to Other Taiwan Destinations\n\nMany travelers use Kaohsiung as a base for exploring southern Taiwan. Here's how to reach popular nearby destinations:\n\n### Kaohsiung to Tainan\n\n**By High-Speed Rail (HSR) from Zuoying Station**:\n\n- Travel time: 20 minutes\n- Cost: NT$250 (US$8)\n- Frequency: Every 15-30 minutes\n- **Best option**: Fastest and most convenient\n\n**By Regular Train from Kaohsiung Main Station**:\n\n- Travel time: 40-50 minutes\n- Cost: NT$120 (US$4)\n- Frequency: Frequent departures\n- **Good for budget travelers**: Half the price of HSR\n\n### Kaohsiung to Kenting\n\n**By Bus (Routes 9117/9188 from Zuoying HSR Station)**:\n\n- Travel time: 2-2.5 hours\n- Cost: NT$350-400 (US$11-13)\n- **Most popular option**: Direct and affordable\n\n**By Private Transfer**:\n\n- Travel time: 1.5-2 hours\n- Cost: NT$2500-3500 (US$80-110)\n- **Best for families/groups**: Door-to-door convenience\n\n### Kaohsiung to Taipei\n\n**By High-Speed Rail (HSR) from Zuoying Station**:\n\n- Travel time: 1.5-2 hours\n- Cost: NT$1490-1765 (US$48-57) depending on train type\n- Frequency: Every 10-30 minutes\n- **Recommended**: Fast, comfortable, scenic\n\n**By Regular Train from Kaohsiung Main Station**:\n\n- Travel time: 4-5.5 hours\n- Cost: NT$843 (US$27)\n- **Budget option**: Takes longer but costs less\n\n### Kaohsiung to Alishan\n\n**By Bus**:\n\n- Travel time: 3-4 hours\n- Cost: NT$450-500 (US$15-16)\n- Requires advance booking and planning\n\n## Essential Kaohsiung Transportation Apps and Cards\n\n### Must-Have Apps\n\n**Google Maps**: Works perfectly in Taiwan for navigation, public transport directions, and finding restaurants. Offline maps recommended.\n\n**MRT Kaohsiung App**: Official app with routes, schedules, and fare information (though Google Maps usually suffices)\n\n**Uber**: For ride-hailing if needed\n\n**Papago or Google Translate**: For translating Chinese text (especially useful for reading menus)\n\n### Essential Transit Cards\n\n**EasyCard (悠遊卡)**:\n\n- Used throughout Taiwan (Kaohsiung, Taipei, other cities)\n- Works on MRT, buses, trains, YouBikes, ferries\n- Accepted at convenience stores (7-Eleven, FamilyMart, Hi-Life, OK Mart)\n- Provides discounts on public transport (about 20% off)\n- Refundable when you leave Taiwan\n- **Cost**: NT$100 card deposit + credit you load (NT$100-500 to start)\n\n**iPASS (一卡通)**:\n\n- Kaohsiung's local transit card (works similarly to EasyCard)\n- Widely accepted in southern Taiwan\n- Same benefits as EasyCard\n- Choose either one—both work well\n\n**MeN Go Card**:\n\n- Tourist pass offering 48 hours of unlimited MRT, Light Rail, and bus travel\n- Also includes discounts at attractions\n- **Cost**: Around NT$700 (US$23)\n- **Worth it if**: You plan to use public transport extensively over two days\n\n**Where to buy**: All available at Kaohsiung Airport MRT station service counter, convenience stores, or major MRT stations.\n\n## Kaohsiung Transportation FAQs\n\n### Is Kaohsiung easy to navigate without knowing Mandarin?\n\n**Yes, absolutely.** Kaohsiung ranks among the most foreigner-friendly cities in Asia. MRT announcements are bilingual, station names are clearly displayed in English, signs at the airport and major areas have English, and younger Taiwanese often speak conversational English. Translation apps help fill any gaps. You won't feel lost or overwhelmed.\n\n### What if my flight arrives after midnight when the MRT is closed?\n\n**Take a taxi or book a private transfer.** Taxis are always available at the airport 24/7. The cost from airport to city centre runs NT$250-400 (US$8-13), which is reasonable for late-night convenience. Alternatively, book a private transfer in advance so a driver is waiting regardless of your arrival time.\n\n### Can I use credit cards on the Kaohsiung MRT?\n\n**No, only cash or transit cards (EasyCard/iPASS).** Ticket machines accept NT$ bills and coins. This is why getting an EasyCard at the airport is highly recommended—you can load it with credit and use it throughout Taiwan without needing exact change.\n\n### Is Kaohsiung Airport far from the city?\n\n**No, it's remarkably close.** Only 8-10 kilometers from central Kaohsiung, meaning 15-30 minutes travel time depending on your destination and transport method. This proximity is one of Kaohsiung's advantages over cities where airports sit an hour outside town.\n\n### Which Kaohsiung MRT station should I go to for the High-Speed Rail to Taipei?\n\n**Zuoying Station (左營站) on the Red Line (R16).** This is Kaohsiung's HSR station, about 24 minutes from the airport on the MRT Red Line direct (no transfers needed). From here, high-speed trains reach Taipei in 1.5-2 hours.\n\n### Can I store luggage at Kaohsiung Airport?\n\n**Yes, baggage storage is available.** The service is on the third floor of the international terminal. Contact: 07-8063366. Useful if you have a long layover and want to explore the city without your bags.\n\n### How do I get from Kaohsiung Airport to Kenting?\n\n**Option 1: Direct bus** (Routes 9117/9117A/9117B) from the airport to Kenting. Purchase tickets at the Pingtung Bus counter in the international arrivals hall near Gate 5. Travel time: 2-2.5 hours. Cost: NT$350-450.\n\n**Option 2: MRT to Zuoying, then bus 9188 to Kenting.** Slightly cheaper but requires a transfer.\n\n**Option 3: Private transfer** (NT$2500-3500) for door-to-door convenience.\n\n### Are there YouBikes (bike rental) at Kaohsiung Airport?\n\n![Kaohsuing-7](https://storage.googleapis.com/lk_blog_prod/images/chris-chai-mHkG2T0uvzE-unsplash.original.jpg)\n\n**Yes.** Kaohsiung's YouBike station is located opposite the airport entrance on Zhongshan 4th Road, accessible via pedestrian crossing. However, biking with luggage isn't practical—this is more useful for locals or if you're staying nearby without bags.\n\n## Final Thoughts: Getting from Kaohsiung Airport to City Centre Made Simple\n\nFor the vast majority of travelers, the **Kaohsiung MRT Red Line** is unquestionably the best way to get from the airport to the city centre. It's fast (15-25 minutes), cheap (NT$30-50 / US$1-1.50), easy to navigate even if you don't speak Mandarin, and runs frequently from early morning until midnight. The airport station connects directly to the arrivals hall—you literally walk from customs to the platform in under 5 minutes.\n\n**Taxis and ride-hailing apps** offer flexibility and door-to-door convenience, especially valuable for late-night arrivals, heavy luggage, or groups. **Private transfers** suit families and those wanting guaranteed pickup. **Buses** work mainly for direct trips to Kenting on a budget.\n\n### The Real Key to Enjoying Kaohsiung\n\nWhat matters most is that Kaohsiung makes arrival incredibly easy—possibly the easiest airport-to-city transfer in Asia. Once you're in the city, that's when the real experience begins.\n\nKaohsiung isn't trying to compete with Taipei's urban energy or Tainan's historical density. This port city offers something different: a more relaxed pace, accessible culture, fantastic food, creative neighborhoods, and genuine friendliness that defines southern Taiwan.\n\nIt's a place best experienced slowly, with local insight, and without rushing between checkboxes on a guidebook list.\n\n### Start Your Kaohsiung Experience the Right Way\n\nYou've figured out how to get from the airport to your hotel. Now what?\n\nThe difference between _seeing_ Kaohsiung and truly _experiencing_ it often comes down to having local guidance—someone who can show you the breakfast spots locals line up at (not just the famous ones), explain why certain neighborhoods feel the way they do, and help you understand Taiwanese culture beyond surface observations.\n\nWhether you're in Kaohsiung for 24 hours or a week, spending even a few hours with a local host at the start of your trip helps everything else make more sense.\n\n👉 [Explore Kaohsiung with a Local](https://lokafy.com/pricing)– Book a private walking tour tailored to your interests and pace.\n\n**Welcome to Kaohsiung. The MRT will get you here in 15 minutes. The city will make you want to stay longer than you planned.** 🌴\n\nEnjoyed this article?\n\nShare on XShare on LinkedIn\n\n## Ready for Your Next Adventure?\n\nJoin thousands of travelers discovering amazing experiences with Lokafy\n\n[Explore Destinations](https://lokafy.com/) [Read More Articles](https://lokafy.com/blog)",

        "summary": "The guide provides detailed information on various transportation methods from Kaohsiung International Airport (KHH) to the city center. Options include:\n\n1. **Kaohsiung MRT**: Fastest and cheapest (15-25 min, NT$30-50). Located directly at the airport, with user-friendly signage in English.\n2. **Taxis**: Convenient for late arrivals and heavy luggage (20-30 min, NT$250-400). Meters used and no tipping expected.\n3. **Ride-hailing apps (Uber)**: Cashless, user-friendly options with slight higher fare (NT$300-450).\n4. **Private transfers**: Offers door-to-door service (25-35 min, NT$1000-1500), ideal for families or large groups.\n5. **Buses**: Generally impractical for city-center travel unless directly going to Kenting (2-2.5 hours, NT$350-450).\n\nKaohsiung Airport is noted for its close proximity to the city (8-10 km), making the MRT an ideal choice for most visitors. The city is vibrant and easy to navigate, with various local attractions and cultural experiences recommended for deeper exploration."

[Ending Your Trip in Kaohsiung?](https://www.newdawn.health/blog/kaohsiung-metro-light-rail-guide-airport-hsr#ending-your-trip-in-kaohsiung)\n10. [Official Planning Links](https://www.newdawn.health/blog/kaohsiung-metro-light-rail-guide-airport-hsr#official-planning-links)\n\n## The Red Line: Airport, Downtown, and HSR\n**Kaohsiung International Airport (R4)** has its own station.\n\n## How to Pay\nKaohsiung also sells one- and two-day QR travel passes, including versions covering both KMRT and KLRT; compare the pass price with the trips you will actually make.\n\n## Why Kaohsiung Is So Easy to Learn\nRed connects airport, center, rail, and HSR. Orange reaches the old port and arts venues.\n\n## Official Planning Links\n- [Kaohsiung Metro - Ticket information](https://www.krtc.com.tw/eng/Ticket/)\n- [Kaohsiung Metro - KMRT and KLRT travel passes](https://www.krtc.com.tw/eng/Ticket/ticket_more?id=1f8ae5a9b7e648bdb58e9ea44cab1d70)\n- [Kaohsiung Metro - Official fare and travel-time search](https://www.krtc.com.tw/eng/)\n\n_Verify current details with Kaohsiung Rapid Transit Corporation._",

        "markdown": "[‹\\\\\nBack to Journal](https://www.newdawn.health/blog)\n\n![](https://www.newdawn.health/svgs/logo-avatar.svg)\n\n**By New Dawn Health Editorial** Editorial Team\n\nShare [Share on Twitter](https://twitter.com/intent/tweet?text=Kaohsiung+Metro+%26+Light+Rail%3A+Airport%2C+HSR+and+Waterfront+Guide&url=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.newdawn.health%2Fblog%2Fkaohsiung-metro-light-rail-guide-airport-hsr \"Share on Twitter\") [Share on LinkedIn](https://www.linkedin.com/sharing/share-offsite/?url=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.newdawn.health%2Fblog%2Fkaohsiung-metro-light-rail-guide-airport-hsr \"Share on LinkedIn\") [Printable version](https://www.newdawn.health/blog/kaohsiung-metro-light-rail-guide-airport-hsr/print \"Printable version\")\n\nIn this essay\n\n01. [The Red Line: Airport, Downtown, and HSR](https://www.newdawn.health/blog/kaohsiung-metro-light-rail-guide-airport-hsr#the-red-line-airport-downtown-and-hsr)\n02. [The Orange Line: Old Port and Culture](https://www.newdawn.health/blog/kaohsiung-metro-light-rail-guide-airport-hsr#the-orange-line-old-port-and-culture)\n03. [The Circular Light Rail](https://www.newdawn.health/blog/kaohsiung-metro-light-rail-guide-airport-hsr#the-circular-light-rail)\n04. [How to Pay](https://www.newdawn.health/blog/kaohsiung-metro-light-rail-guide-airport-hsr#how-to-pay)\n05. [Three Easy Kaohsiung Itineraries](https://www.newdawn.health/blog/kaohsiung-metro-light-rail-guide-airport-hsr#three-easy-kaohsiung-itineraries)\n06. [Heat Changes the Last Kilometer](https://www.newdawn.health/blog/kaohsiung-metro-light-rail-guide-airport-hsr#heat-changes-the-last-kilometer)\n07. [Accessibility and Events](https://www.newdawn.health/blog/kaohsiung-metro-light-rail-guide-airport-hsr#accessibility-and-events)\n08. [Why Kaohsiung Is So Easy to Learn](https://www.newdawn.health/blog/kaohsiung-metro-light-rail-guide-airport-hsr#why-kaohsiung-is-so-easy-to-learn)\n09. [Ending Your Trip in Kaohsiung?](https://www.newdawn.health/blog/kaohsiung-metro-light-rail-guide-airport-hsr#ending-your-trip-in-kaohsiung)\n10. [Official Planning Links](https://www.newdawn.health/blog/kaohsiung-metro-light-rail-guide-airport-hsr#official-planning-links)\n\nKaohsiung gives visitors a transit map that matches the city's shape. One metro line runs north-south from the airport through downtown to the high-speed rail connection and beyond. Another crosses east-west through the cultural districts. Around the waterfront, a circular light rail glides past piers, parks, museums, markets, and neighborhoods that once faced industrial tracks.\n\nThe result is not a huge network, but it is exceptionally legible. Learn one red line, one orange line, and one light-rail loop, and most first-time itineraries become easy.\n\n## The Red Line: Airport, Downtown, and HSR\n\nThe Red Line is the visitor's backbone. **Kaohsiung International Airport (R4)** has its own station. Northbound trains reach Sanduo Shopping District, Central Park, Formosa Boulevard, Kaohsiung Main Station, and **Zuoying (R16)**, the transfer for Taiwan High Speed Rail.\n\nThis direct airport-to-city connection is one of Kaohsiung's great travel advantages. You do not need an airport express ticket; use the regular metro system. Check the final train before a late arrival and leave enough time for airline check-in on departure day.\n\n## The Orange Line: Old Port and Culture\n\nThe Orange Line runs from Hamasen through Yanchengpu and Formosa Boulevard toward the east. Visitors use it for:\n\n- **Hamasen (O1)** \\- old port district and light-rail connections.\n- **Yanchengpu (O2)** \\- Pier-2 Art Center access, local food, and waterfront walks.\n- **Formosa Boulevard (O5/R10)** \\- the only transfer between red and orange, famous for the Dome of Light.\n- **Weiwuying (O10)** \\- National Kaohsiung Center for the Arts.\n- **Dadong (O13)** \\- arts facilities and Fongshan exploration.\n\nStation numbering skips O3 because of network planning history; you have not missed a stop.\n\n## The Circular Light Rail\n\nKaohsiung Light Rail has operated as a complete 38-station loop since the full circle began service on New Year's Day 2024. It serves places the two heavy-rail lines approach less directly: Dream Mall, the exhibition center, Cruise Terminal, Glory Pier, Love Pier, Pier-2, Hamasen, Shoushan, the Museum of Fine Arts, and Science and Technology Museum corridor.\n\nThe vehicles run at street level, so they are slower than the MRT and more affected by intersections. That is part of their usefulness: the light rail is transport and a low-speed city tour.\n\n**Tap when entering and tap when leaving.** Platforms may feel open compared with gated metro stations, but the ride is not free. Since 26 February 2024, fares have been distance-based: NT$20 for the first 5 km, rising in NT$5 steps to a maximum of NT$35 per trip, with a discount for EasyCard, iPASS and QR-code riders. Inspectors can check proof of payment.\n\n## How to Pay\n\nEasyCard and iPASS are the easiest choices across metro and light rail. Single-journey tickets are available for the metro. Kaohsiung also sells one- and two-day QR travel passes, including versions covering both KMRT and KLRT; compare the pass price with the trips you will actually make.\n\nOne card is for one passenger. Always use the same card or payment credential to enter and exit. If a light-rail reader fails, use another reader or ask staff rather than repeatedly tapping and hoping the transaction registered.\n\n## Three Easy Kaohsiung Itineraries\n\n- **Waterfront day:** Orange Line to Yanchengpu, explore Pier-2, then use light rail around Love Pier, Glory Pier, and the harbor.\n- **Arrival day:** Red Line from the airport to Central Park or Formosa Boulevard, leave luggage, then continue by orange line for an evening in Yancheng.\n- **North-to-south transfer:** HSR to Zuoying, Red Line into downtown, then continue to the airport on the same line when the trip ends.\n\nFor Cijin, take the metro or light rail toward Hamasen and continue to the ferry at Gushan. The ferry is a separate fare and part of the experience.\n\n## Heat Changes the Last Kilometer\n\nKaohsiung sidewalks and waterfronts are enjoyable, but summer heat and sudden rain make distances feel longer. Choose the station exit closest to the destination, use YouBike for short flat trips if you are comfortable in traffic, and take a taxi when luggage, children, or midday heat make a nominal fifteen-minute walk unreasonable.\n\nKeep water with you but do not drink in the metro paid area. Eating, drinking, smoking, and chewing gum are prohibited there. Light-rail platform rules and onboard etiquette still apply even though the stations are open to the street.\n\n## Accessibility and Events\n\nMetro stations have elevators and accessible gates; light-rail vehicles offer low-floor boarding. At large concerts, exhibitions, and holiday waterfront events, platforms can become crowded and services may be adjusted. Use the operator's live information, allow an extra train, and do not wait until the last possible departure.\n\nTyphoon-day schedules can change substantially. If local government suspends work and classes, check official announcements before leaving the hotel.\n\n## Why Kaohsiung Is So Easy to Learn\n\nTaipei's system is powerful because it offers many choices. Kaohsiung's is powerful because the choices are obvious. Red connects airport, center, rail, and HSR. Orange reaches the old port and arts venues. Light rail traces the waterfront and closes the circle.\n\nBy the second day, visitors stop consulting the entire map. They ask one question: red, orange, or the loop? In a broad subtropical city, that simplicity gives more time to look out the window.\n\n## Ending Your Trip in Kaohsiung?\n\nIf your Taiwan trip starts in Taipei and ends in the south, the Taipei days are the slot for a preventive check-up: **Convenient**, our 1.5-hour holistic exam at iHope Clinic in Taipei, is **$299**, with an English report. See the [screening packages](https://www.newdawn.health/health-screening) to fit one around the rest of the itinerary.\n\n## Official Planning Links\n\n- [Kaohsiung Metro - MRT and Light Rail station list](https://www.krtc.com.tw/eng/Guide/timetable)\n- [Kaohsiung Metro - Ticket information](https://www.krtc.com.tw/eng/Ticket/)\n- [Kaohsiung Metro - KMRT and KLRT travel passes](https://www.krtc.com.tw/eng/Ticket/ticket_more?id=1f8ae5a9b7e648bdb58e9ea44cab1d70)\n- [Kaohsiung Metro - Official fare and travel-time search](https://www.krtc.com.tw/eng/)\n- [Central News Agency - Kaohsiung light rail switches to distance-based fares: NT$20 first 5 km, NT$5 per 2 km, NT$35 maximum (2024-02-22; accessed 2026-10-14)](https://www.cna.com.tw/news/aloc/202402220127.aspx)\n- [Central News Agency - Kaohsiung Circular Light Rail full loop opens on New Year's Day 2024 (2023-12-31; accessed 2026-10-14)](https://www.cna.com.tw/news/ahel/202312310028.aspx)\n\n_Fares, passes, schedules, event operations, and accepted payments can change. Verify current details with Kaohsiung Rapid Transit Corporation._\n\n[![Taiwan Adventist Hospital: What Foreign Patients Should Know](https://www.newdawn.health/images/blog-covers/blog-211.webp)\\\\\n\\\\\nTravel Guide\\\\\n\\\\\n**Taiwan Adventist Hospital: What Foreign Patients Should Know**\\\\\n\\\\\nSeptember 29, 2026 · 7 min readRead →](https://www.newdawn.health/blog/taiwan-adventist-hospital-what-foreign-patients-should-know)[![Tipping in Taiwan: Why Nobody Wants Your 20% (and What the 10% Service Charge Really Is)](https://www.newdawn.health/images/blog-covers/blog-183.webp)\\\\\n\\\\\nTravel Guide\\\\\n\\\\\n**Tipping in Taiwan: Why Nobody Wants Your 20% (and What the 10% Service Charge Really Is)**\\\\\n\\\\\nSeptember 02, 2026 · 9 min readRead →](https://www.newdawn.health/blog/tipping-in-taiwan-no-tipping-culture-service-charge)[![Why You Can Leave Your Phone on the Table in Taiwan](https://www.newdawn.health/images/blog-covers/blog-184.webp)\\\\\n\\\\\nTravel Guide\\\\\n\\\\\n**Why You Can Leave Your Phone on the Table in Taiwan**\\\\\n\\\\\nSeptember 04, 2026 · 9 min readRead →](https://www.newdawn.health/blog/taiwan-safety-culture-trust-phone-on-table)\n\nReady when you are\n\n## Curious about your baseline?\n\nA thirty-minute consultation with our concierge team is the fastest way to know whether preventive imaging is right for you.\n\n[Explore packages →](https://www.newdawn.health/health-screening)",

        "summary": "The article provides an overview of the Kaohsiung Metro and Light Rail system, highlighting its accessibility and user-friendly structure. Key points include: 1) **Red Line**: Connects the airport to downtown and HSR, simplifying travel for visitors. 2) **Orange Line**: Runs through cultural districts, linking various art and food hubs. 3) **Circular Light Rail**: A new 38-station loop that serves key waterfront locations, offering a scenic ride. 4) **Payment Options**: Emphasizes using EasyCard and QR travel passes for convenience. 5) **Three Suggested Itineraries**: Plans for effective use of the transit system for exploring Kaohsiung. 6) **Accessibility Features**: Includes elevators and low-floor boarding, with considerations for crowded events. The article concludes that Kaohsiung's transit simplicity enhances the travel experience."

        "url": "https://www.travelwithhello.com/blog/taiwan-transport-guide",
        "title": "Getting Around Taiwan: Transport Guide for Travellers",
        "description": "# Getting Around Taiwan: Transport Guide for Travellers\n## Taiwan Airport Transfers: From Plane to City Center\nAs of 2026, one-way fares are typically NT$150–NT$160 (around $5 USD), making it the best value option for solo travellers or couples.\n\nFrom Kaohsiung International Airport, the Kaohsiung MRT Red Line connects directly to downtown in about 15–20 minutes, with fares around NT$35–NT$50 ($1–$2 USD).\n\n## Inter-City Travel in Taiwan: High-Speed Rail, TRA, and Buses\n| HSR | Taipei–Kaohsiung | 1.5–2 hours | NT$1,500–1,600 | Speed and comfort | | Intercity Bus | Taipei–Taichung | 2–3 hours | NT$250–400 | Budget travel |\n\n## Common Questions About Getting Around Taiwan (Q&A)\nYes.",
        "position": 4,
        "markdown": "Getting Around8 min read29 May 2026\n\n# Getting Around Taiwan: Transport Guide for Travellers\n\nAirport transfers, public transport, ride-hailing apps, inter-city travel, and driving tips for Taiwan.\n\nBy Hello Travel Team\n\nThis article contains affiliate links. If you book through them, Hello may earn a commission at no additional cost to you. Our editorial recommendations are independent. [Learn more](https://www.travelwithhello.com/affiliate-disclosure).\n\n![Getting Around Taiwan: Transport Guide for Travellers](https://images.unsplash.com/photo-1745211516793-df8ed8e8ecd5)\n\n## TL;DR: How to Get Around Taiwan Efficiently\n\nGetting around Taiwan is easy, affordable, and traveller-friendly, thanks to excellent public transport, clear English signage, and simple payment cards like EasyCard and iPASS. Most visitors mix metro, buses, and trains, with taxis or ride-hailing for late nights or airport transfers.\n\nThink of Taiwan as a place where you can land, tap a single card, and move seamlessly between city metro lines, rural buses, and high-speed trains. Taiwan’s main cities are compact, public transport is frequent, and airport transfers are straightforward even if it’s your first visit. In 2023, Taiwan welcomed over 6.4 million international visitors according to Taiwan’s Tourism Bureau, many of whom rely almost entirely on public transport during their stays.\n\nFor most travellers, the ideal Taiwan transport strategy is:\n\n- Use metro and buses for daily sightseeing\n- Take Taiwan High Speed Rail (HSR) or express trains for inter-city travel\n- Grab taxis or ride-hailing for late nights or luggage-heavy trips\n- Rent a car or scooter only if you’re comfortable driving and have the right licence\n\nReliable mobile data makes navigation, translation, and ride-hailing much smoother. An eSIM from Hello lets you arrive in Taiwan already connected, so you can instantly use Google Maps, local ride-hailing apps, and translate station signs without hunting for a SIM counter.\n\n## Taiwan Airport Transfers: From Plane to City Center\n\nTaiwan’s major airports offer reliable, budget-friendly transfers, with most travellers choosing airport MRT trains or buses over taxis thanks to clear signage and fixed, predictable prices. You can usually reach city centres in under an hour for less than $10 USD as of 2026.\n\nThe busiest gateway, Taoyuan International Airport (for Taipei), is extremely well-connected. The Taoyuan Airport MRT runs every 15 minutes, taking about 35–40 minutes to Taipei Main Station. As of 2026, one-way fares are typically NT$150–NT$160 (around $5 USD), making it the best value option for solo travellers or couples. Bilingual signage and platform announcements make transfers straightforward even for first-time visitors.\n\nTaxis from Taoyuan Airport to central Taipei cost roughly NT$1,100–NT$1,300 ($35–$42 USD) in 2026, depending on traffic and your exact destination. Most cabs are metered and accept cash; some accept contactless payment, but not all. This is a good choice if you’re arriving late at night or carrying bulky luggage.\n\nFrom Kaohsiung International Airport, the Kaohsiung MRT Red Line connects directly to downtown in about 15–20 minutes, with fares around NT$35–NT$50 ($1–$2 USD). Taichung’s airport is less busy and relies mainly on airport buses and taxis.\n\nFor smooth airport transfers, download the Hello app and activate a Hello eSIM before landing. Being online immediately helps you check MRT timetables, call taxis, and share your live location with friends without scrambling for airport Wi‑Fi.\n\n## Taiwan Public Transport Guide: Metro, Bus, and Regional Rail\n\nTaiwan’s public transport system is one of the most efficient in Asia, with metro, bus, and rail networks that are clean, punctual, and well-signposted in English, making it easy for travellers to get around Taiwan without renting a car.\n\nTaipei, Kaohsiung, and Taichung each have modern metro systems that cover major tourist areas. Taipei Metro alone carries over 2 million passenger trips per day according to Taipei Rapid Transit Corporation, and is known for being safe, air-conditioned, and impeccably clean. Single rides in Taipei typically cost NT$20–NT$65 ($0.70–$2.10 USD) in 2026, depending on distance.\n\nBuses fill in the gaps, connecting metro stations with neighbourhoods and tourist sites like Jiufen or Yehliu. Fares in cities usually start around NT$15–NT$30 ($0.50–$1 USD). Outside cities, intercity buses run frequently between major hubs, often with Wi‑Fi and comfortable seating.\n\nThe backbone of regional travel is **TRA (Taiwan Railways Administration)**, which operates conventional trains circling the island. These trains are slower than high-speed rail but stop at more local stations, ideal for exploring smaller towns along the east coast, such as Hualien or Taitung.\n\nFor city transport, most travellers use **EasyCard** or **iPASS**, rechargeable contactless smart cards accepted on metro, buses, and many trains, plus some convenience stores. Cards cost around NT$100–NT$150 ($3–$5 USD) plus stored value, and can be bought at metro stations and 7‑Eleven or FamilyMart.\n\nHere’s a quick comparison of Taiwan’s main public transport options:\n\n\\| Mode \\| Best For \\| Typical Cost (2026) \\| Pros \\| Cons \\|\n\\| Metro \\| City sightseeing \\| NT$20–65 per ride \\| Fast, clean, English signage \\| Limited late-night service \\|\n\\| City Bus \\| Short city trips \\| NT$15–30 per ride \\| Wide coverage, cheap \\| Routes can be confusing \\|\n\\| TRA Train \\| Regional stops \\| NT$100–600+ per trip \\| Scenic, many stations \\| Slower than HSR \\|\n\n## Passes and Transport Cards: EasyCard, iPASS, and Tickets\n\nThe easiest way to use Taiwan public transport is with a contactless smart card like EasyCard or iPASS, which let you tap into metros, buses, and some trains without worrying about buying separate tickets or exact change.\n\nEasyCard is the most common card in Taipei and northern Taiwan, while iPASS is popular in Kaohsiung and the south, but both work across most of the island. You buy them at metro station counters, ticket machines, or convenience stores for around NT$100–NT$150 ($3–$5 USD) in 2026, then top up as needed. They can also be used for bike rentals, some parking lots, and small purchases at convenience stores.\n\nFor intensive sightseeing in Taipei, the **Taipei Metro offers day passes**, such as a 24‑hour pass around NT$180 ($6 USD), giving unlimited metro rides within the time period. This can be great value if you expect to take four or more metro trips in a day between areas like Ximending, Shilin, and Taipei 101.\n\nOn regional routes, TRA trains and the Taiwan High Speed Rail (HSR) primarily use reserved-seat tickets, which you can buy online, via machines, or at station counters. Tourist passes exist, such as HSR multi-day passes or rail touring passes, but these are most beneficial if you plan multiple long-distance trips in a short time.\n\nTo maximise flexibility:\n\n- Use EasyCard or iPASS for daily city travel\n- Combine with individual tickets for long-distance trains\n- Carry some cash for rural buses or small towns where cards may not be accepted\n\nWith a Hello eSIM active, it’s easy to check live route planners, metro maps, or bus arrival times on local apps and Google Maps, so you can decide on the best pass or card based on your daily plans.\n\n## Inter-City Travel in Taiwan: High-Speed Rail, TRA, and Buses\n\nThe fastest way to travel between major cities in Taiwan is the Taiwan High Speed Rail (HSR), while TRA trains and intercity buses offer cheaper, more scenic options that stop at smaller towns and coastal areas.\n\nTaiwan HSR runs along the west coast from Taipei to Kaohsiung, linking cities like Taoyuan, Hsinchu, Taichung, and Tainan. Journey times are impressive: Taipei–Kaohsiung takes about 1.5–2 hours, compared to 4–5 hours on conventional trains. As of 2026, standard reserved seats for Taipei–Taichung typically cost around NT$700–NT$800 ($23–$26 USD), while Taipei–Kaohsiung fares are roughly NT$1,500–NT$1,600 ($50–$53 USD). According to Taiwan High Speed Rail Corporation, trains run every 15–30 minutes during peak times.\n\nTRA (Taiwan Railways Administration) trains follow a loop around the island, including the scenic east coast. Services range from slower local trains to faster express services such as Tze-Chiang Limited Express. Fares are generally lower than HSR; for example, Taipei–Hualien on an express train might cost around NT$440–NT$540 ($15–$18 USD) and take about 2–3 hours.\n\nIntercity buses, offered by companies like Kuo-Kuang, Ubus, and others, are often the cheapest option, especially on popular routes between Taipei, Taichung, and southern cities. Expect modern coaches with air conditioning and, on many routes, onboard Wi‑Fi.\n\nHere’s a quick comparison of inter-city options:\n\n\\| Mode \\| Route Example \\| Time (Approx.) \\| Typical Cost (2026) \\| Best For \\|\n\\| HSR \\| Taipei–Kaohsiung \\| 1.5–2 hours \\| NT$1,500–1,600 \\| Speed and comfort \\|\n\\| TRA Express \\| Taipei–Hualien \\| 2–3 hours \\| NT$440–540 \\| Scenic east coast \\|\n\\| Intercity Bus \\| Taipei–Taichung \\| 2–3 hours \\| NT$250–400 \\| Budget travel \\|\n\nBooking in advance is wise on weekends and public holidays. With Hello’s trip planning and budget tracking features, you can log HSR, bus, and train booking costs as you go and keep your daily transport spend in check.\n\n## Ride-Hailing, Taxis, and Staying Connected with Hello eSIM\n\nRide-hailing and taxis are widely available in Taiwan’s major cities, offering a convenient backup to public transport—especially at night, with luggage, or when you’re travelling with a group and want door-to-door service.\n\nTraditional **yellow taxis** are everywhere in Taipei, Taichung, and Kaohsiung. Flagfall in Taipei as of 2026 is typically around NT$85–100 ($2.80–$3.30 USD), with additional charges per kilometer and time. A 15–20 minute ride within central Taipei often costs NT$200–NT$300 ($6.50–$10 USD), depending on traffic. Most drivers accept cash; some accept contactless payment.\n\nRide-hailing apps operate alongside taxis. Uber is the most commonly used international platform in Taipei and other big cities, often partnering with licensed taxi fleets. Local apps (such as Taiwan Taxi’s own app) also provide app-based booking and fare estimates, but they may be more Chinese-language focused, so having online translation handy helps.\n\nBecause ride-hailing and navigation apps require mobile data, it’s best to sort connectivity before you land. A Hello eSIM for Taiwan lets you:\n\n- Arrive already online, so you can book an Uber or taxi at the airport\n- Use live maps and traffic when choosing between MRT, bus, or car\n- Share your ride details and location with friends\n\nHello’s expense splitting feature is also useful when you’re dividing taxi fares among friends in different currencies. The app can automatically convert amounts based on live exchange rates and track who owes what, turning a night of shared rides into clean, transparent trip spending records.\n\nFor context, Taiwan had over 24 million domestic tourist trips and millions of international visitors annually before the pandemic; as tourism recovers according to Taiwan’s Tourism Bureau, ride-hailing coverage continues to expand in key visitor hotspots like Taipei, Taichung, Tainan, and Kaohsiung.\n\n## Driving, Scooter Rental, and Road Tips in Taiwan\n\nDriving in Taiwan gives you the freedom to reach remote hot springs, mountain roads, and coastal villages, but it’s best suited to confident drivers who understand local rules and have the right licence and insurance.\n\nTo legally drive in Taiwan, most visitors need either an International Driving Permit (IDP) based on their home licence or a licence from a country with a recognised agreement. For car rentals, major agencies typically require you to be at least 21, hold a valid licence, and provide a credit card. Daily car rental rates in 2026 commonly range from NT$1,800–NT$3,000 ($60–$100 USD), depending on the car class, season, and pickup location.\n\nScooters are popular with locals, and rental shops operate in cities and tourist regions like Hualien or Kenting. However, scooter accidents involving tourists are not uncommon, so only rent if you’re very comfortable riding and aware of local traffic patterns. Scooter rentals might cost around NT$400–NT$800 ($13–$26 USD) per day.\n\nRoad rules are similar to many other countries: people drive on the right, seatbelts are mandatory, and drunk driving penalties are strict. Urban driving can be busy, with many scooters; rural roads can be narrow and winding. Typhoon season and heavy rain may affect road conditions, especially in the mountains.\n\nMany travellers still prefer public transport for most of their trip and only rent a car for specific legs, such as exploring Taroko Gorge or the east coast. If you do drive, using online maps and checking real-time traffic is essential. With a Hello eSIM, you can keep maps, weather alerts, and language translation tools running in the background, ensuring safer navigation and fewer wrong turns.\n\n## Common Questions About Getting Around Taiwan (Q&A)\n\nMost travellers can get around Taiwan easily using public transport, with metro, buses, and trains covering virtually all major sights, while taxis and ride-hailing fill in gaps for late nights, airport transfers, and luggage-heavy journeys.\n\n**Q: Is Taiwan public transport foreigner-friendly?**\n\nYes. Major systems like Taipei Metro, Kaohsiung MRT, and HSR have English signage, bilingual announcements, and clear route maps. Ticket machines usually offer English, and staff at bigger stations can often help with basic questions.\n\n**Q: How much should I budget for daily transport in 2026?**\n\nFor a typical day in Taipei, expect around NT$150–300 ($5–$10 USD) for metro and buses if you’re sightseeing actively, plus extra if you take taxis. Inter-city days involving HSR or express trains can push your transport spend to NT$1,000–2,000 ($33–$66 USD).\n\n**Q: Do I really need an EasyCard or iPASS?**\n\nThey’re not mandatory, but highly recommended. You’ll save time, avoid buying individual tickets, and sometimes benefit from small fare discounts. They also work for multiple modes, including metro, buses, some trains, and even YouBike rentals.\n\n**Q: Are taxis and Uber affordable?**\n\nCompared with many Western cities, yes. A typical 15–20 minute ride in central Taipei is often NT$200–300 ($6.50–$10 USD), making taxis practical for groups or late-night journeys.\n\n**Q: Do I need mobile data to get around?**\n\nYou can survive with offline maps, but live navigation, bus timings, and ride-hailing are much easier with mobile data. Activating a Hello eSIM before your trip lets you arrive connected and instantly use transport apps without queuing for a physical SIM.\n\n## Explore These Destinations\n\n[Taiwan](https://www.travelwithhello.com/taiwan)\n\n## Stay Connected\n\n### 🇹🇼 eSIM Plans for Taiwan\n\n[5 GB\\\\\n\\\\\n30 days · Xie Xie\\\\\n\\\\\n$12.00\\\\\n\\\\\nUSD](https://www.travelwithhello.com/esim/taiwan) [Unlimited\\\\\n\\\\\n3 days · Xie Xie\\\\\n\\\\\n$12.50\\\\\n\\\\\nUSD](https://www.travelwithhello.com/esim/taiwan) [10 GB\\\\\n\\\\\n30 days · Xie Xie\\\\\n\\\\\n$19.00\\\\\n\\\\\nUSD](https://www.travelwithhello.com/esim/taiwan)\n\n[View all plans for Taiwan→](https://www.travelwithhello.com/taiwan#connectivity)\n\nBook this trip\n\n## Compare options on trusted travel sites\n\nPlan car hire for this trip\n\nContinue to a reviewed booking partner to check current vehicle availability, pickup details, and terms.\n\n[![Expedia](https://www.travelwithhello.com/partners/expedia.svg)Browse car hire on ExpediaView →](https://expedia.com/affiliate?siteid=1&landingPage=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.expedia.com.sg%2FDestinations-In-Taiwan.d176.Car-Hire-Destinations&camref=1101l5PSZt&creativeref=1100l86809&adref=PZZIThDPVq)\n\n## Stay connected in Taiwan\n\nGet an eSIM before you land. Hello gives you instant data in 200+ countries — no SIM swaps, no roaming fees.\n\n[Download on the App Store](https://apps.apple.com/app/hello-trip-expense-tracker/id6758774598)\n\n## Related Articles\n\n[![Taiwan Safety Guide: Tips for a Safe Trip](https://images.unsplash.com/photo-1745211516793-df8ed8e8ecd5)\\\\\n\\\\\nSafety & Health8 min read\\\\\n\\\\\n**Taiwan Safety Guide: Tips for a Safe Trip** \\\\\n\\\\\nSafety tips, health advisories, emergency contacts, common scams, and travel insurance advice for Taiwan.\\\\\n\\\\\n22 July 2026](https://www.travelwithhello.com/blog/taiwan-safety-travel-guide) [![Taiwan Visa & Entry Requirements for Singapore Passport Holders](https://images.unsplash.com/photo-1745211516793-df8ed8e8ecd5)\\\\\n\\\\\nVisa & Entry8 min read\\\\\n\\\\\n**Taiwan Visa & Entry Requirements for Singapore Passport Holders** \\\\\n\\\\\nVisa requirements, passport validity, customs allowances, and entry tips for Singapore citizens travelling to Taiwan.\\\\\n\\\\\n13 July 2026](https://www.travelwithhello.com/blog/taiwan-visa-entry-guide) [![eSIM for Taiwan: Best Plans, Coverage, and Setup Tips](https://images.unsplash.com/photo-1745211516793-df8ed8e8ecd5)\\\\\n\\\\\neSIM & Connectivity8 min read\\\\\n\\\\\n**eSIM for Taiwan: Best Plans, Coverage, and Setup Tips** \\\\\n\\\\\nEverything you need to know about using eSIM in Taiwan. Best data plans, network coverage, and setup instructions.\\\\\n\\\\\n4 July 2026](https://www.travelwithhello.com/blog/esim-taiwan-guide)\n\n[← Back to all articles](https://www.travelwithhello.com/blog)",

        "summary": "The article provides a comprehensive guide for travelers on how to navigate Taiwan using various transportation options. Key points include: \n\n1. **Overview of Transportation**: Taiwan's public transport is efficient, affordable, and tourist-friendly, featuring metro systems, buses, and trains. The use of contactless cards like EasyCard and iPASS simplifies travel. \n\n2. **Airport Transfers**: Major airports offer economical transfers to city centers via MRT trains or buses, with clear signage and reasonable fares. The Taoyuan Airport MRT is highlighted for its efficiency. \n\n3. **Public Transport**: Major cities such as Taipei, Kaohsiung, and Taichung have extensive metro networks. Buses and TRA trains supplement the metro with wider coverage and scenic routes. \n\n4. **Transport Cards**: EasyCard and iPASS are recommended for easy access to public transport and some retail purchases. Special passes are available for extensive use within cities. \n\n5. **Inter-City Travel**: High-Speed Rail (HSR) is the fastest option for traveling between cities, while TRA trains and intercity buses offer scenic alternatives. \n\n6. **Ride-Hailing and Taxis**: Widely available in urban areas, they provide convenient travel options, especially late at night. \n\n7. **Driving**: While driving is an option for reaching remote areas, it requires confidence and appropriate licensing. Safety guidelines are mentioned. \n\n8. **Connectivity**: Obtaining a Hello eSIM is encouraged for seamless navigation and communication. \n\n9. **FAQs** address common traveler concerns regarding public transport accessibility, budgeting, and mobile data needs in Taiwan."

Airport\n\n[Kaohsiung International Driving to the Airport\n### Domestic Terminal\n#### To Domestic Terminal:\n3. Turn left into the Kaohsiung Airport main entrance.\n\n### Int'l Terminal Arrival (1F)\n#### To Int'l Terminal Arrival:\n1. Go straight along Zhongshan 4th Road.\n3. Turn left into the Kaohsiung Airport main entrance.\n\n## Kaohsiung MRT (Metro)\n- The airport is served by **Kaohsiung International Airport Station (R4)** on the Red Line. Exit 2 connects to the Domestic Terminal; Exit 6 connects to the International Terminal. Four other exits are located within the parking area.\n- **Frequency:** 4-6 minutes during peak hours; no more than 8 minutes during off-peak hours.\n\n[MRT Real-time Info](https://www.krtc.com.tw/Eng/Guide/station_info?id=0a444bb0f2eb4d0a88f451bdaf585fcc)",

Google\")\n\n## International Terminal Map\n\n### 1F — Arrivals Level\n\n![International Terminal 1F Floor Plan](https://www.kia.gov.tw/images/trans/floor/International1F.jpg)\n\n### Exit Area\n\n- **Left of exit (near restrooms):** Convenience store, Bank\n- **Right of exit:** National Immigration Agency counter, Bank, Council of Labor Affairs Foreign Worker Service Station\n\n### Arrivals Hall\n\n- **Both sides:** ATMs, Duty-free merchandise service counter, Bank\n- **Right side:** Baggage service, Passenger service centre\n- **In front of Immigration counter:** Passport control\n\n### Arrival Process\n\n- Arrivals Hall → **Customs inspection**\n- After Customs: **Aviation service desk**\n\n### Ground Transportation\n\n- **In front of exit:** Car park (private vehicles)\n- **Right of exit:** Coach/bus car park, MRT station, Intercity bus, Car rental centre\n\n### 3F — Departures Level\n\n![International Terminal 3F Floor Plan](https://www.kia.gov.tw/images/trans/floor/International3F.jpg)\n\n### Entrance Area\n\n- Customs & Tourism Association Service Desk\n- Insurance & Telecom Services\n- Post Office\n- Information desk\n- Bank\n\n### Check-in Zone\n\n- **Centre:** Check-in counters\n- **Left side:** Centers for Disease Control, Fast food restaurant, Baggage service\n- **Right side:** Shops, Free Wi-Fi area\n- **Both sides:** Airline offices\n\n### After Security (Airside)\n\n- Baggage screening & Passport control\n- **Both sides:** VIP lounges\n- **Left side:** Business centre\n- **Right side (past VIP lounge):** Nursing room, Animal & Plant Quarantine office\n\n### Duty-Free Shopping Area\n\nAvailable on both sides past the VIP lounges:\n\n- Information desks, Tobacco & Liquor Corporation, Duty-free shops, Luxury brand boutiques\n- Nursing room, Snack bar, National Museum of History shop, Massage services\n- Prayer room, Free Wi-Fi area, Mobile phone charging stations\n- **Right side only:** Children's play area, Cultural gallery\n\n## Domestic Terminal Facilities\n\n### 1F\n\n![Domestic Terminal 1F Floor Plan](https://www.kia.gov.tw/images/trans/floor/Domestic_EN.jpg)\n\n### Entrance Area\n\n- **Entrance:** Passenger service centre\n- **Left of entrance:** Food & beverage area\n\n### Check-in Area\n\n- **Right from entrance:** Daily Air Corporation & UNI Air service counters\n- **Past airline counters:** Luggage lockers, Baggage drop area\n\n### Transfer to International Terminal\n\nAccess the International Terminal via the left side of the entrance.\n\n## Driving to the Airport\n\n### Domestic Terminal\n\n![Driving route to Domestic Terminal 1](https://www.kia.gov.tw/images/map_dom/t-GoIn_map_a_01.jpg)![Driving route to Domestic Terminal 2](https://www.kia.gov.tw/images/map_dom/t-GoIn_map_a_02.jpg)![Driving route to Domestic Terminal 3](https://www.kia.gov.tw/images/map_dom/t-GoIn_map_a_03.jpg)![Driving route to Domestic Terminal 4](https://www.kia.gov.tw/images/map_dom/t-GoIn_map_a_04.jpg)![Driving route to Domestic Terminal 5](https://www.kia.gov.tw/images/map_dom/t-GoIn_map_a_05.jpg)![Driving route to Domestic Terminal 6](https://www.kia.gov.tw/images/map_dom/t-GoIn_map_a_06.jpg)![Driving route to Domestic Terminal 7](https://www.kia.gov.tw/images/map_dom/t-GoIn_map_a_07.jpg)![Driving route to Domestic Terminal 8](https://www.kia.gov.tw/images/map_dom/t-GoIn_map_a_08.jpg)![Driving route to Domestic Terminal 9](https://www.kia.gov.tw/images/map_dom/t-GoIn_map_a_09.jpg)![Driving route to Domestic Terminal 10](https://www.kia.gov.tw/images/map_dom/t-GoIn_map_a_10.jpg)\n\n#### To Domestic Terminal:\n\n1. Go straight along Zhongshan 4th Road.\n2. Slow down at curves.\n3. Turn left into the Kaohsiung Airport main entrance.\n4. Follow the signs for \"Domestic Terminal\" to arrive.\n\n#### Leaving for Downtown:\n\n1. Go straight from the Domestic entrance/exit.\n2. Head towards the International Terminal Arrival route.\n3. Pass the International Terminal following the signs.\n4. Pass under the viaduct and follow signs to Zhongshan 4th Road towards downtown.\n\n### Int'l Terminal Departure (3F)\n\n![Driving route to International Departure 1](https://www.kia.gov.tw/images/map_inl_dep/GoIn_map_c_01.jpg)![Driving route to International Departure 2](https://www.kia.gov.tw/images/map_inl_dep/GoIn_map_c_02.jpg)![Driving route to International Departure 3](https://www.kia.gov.tw/images/map_inl_dep/GoIn_map_c_03.jpg)![Driving route to International Departure 4](https://www.kia.gov.tw/images/map_inl_dep/GoIn_map_c_04.jpg)![Driving route to International Departure 5](https://www.kia.gov.tw/images/map_inl_dep/GoIn_map_c_05.jpg)![Driving route to International Departure 6](https://www.kia.gov.tw/images/map_inl_dep/GoIn_map_c_06.jpg)![Driving route to International Departure 7](https://www.kia.gov.tw/images/map_inl_dep/GoIn_map_c_07.jpg)![Driving route to International Departure 8](https://www.kia.gov.tw/images/map_inl_dep/GoIn_map_c_08.jpg)![Driving route to International Departure 9](https://www.kia.gov.tw/images/map_inl_dep/GoIn_map_c_09.jpg)\n\n#### To Int'l Terminal Departure:\n\n1. Go straight along Zhongshan 4th Road.\n2. Slow down at curves.\n3. Turn left into the Kaohsiung Airport main entrance.\n4. Take the ramp up to the viaduct and go straight.\n5. Follow \"International Departure\" signs to reach the 3rd floor.\n\n#### Leaving for Downtown:\n\n1. Follow the signs outside the terminal.\n2. Pass under the viaduct and follow signs to Zhongshan 4th Road towards downtown.\n\n### Int'l Terminal Arrival (1F)\n\n![Driving route to International Arrival 1](https://www.kia.gov.tw/images/map_inl_arr/GoIn_map_b_01.jpg)![Driving route to International Arrival 2](https://www.kia.gov.tw/images/map_inl_arr/GoIn_map_b_02.jpg)![Driving route to International Arrival 3](https://www.kia.gov.tw/images/map_inl_arr/GoIn_map_b_03.jpg)![Driving route to International Arrival 4](https://www.kia.gov.tw/images/map_inl_arr/GoIn_map_b_04.jpg)![Driving route to International Arrival 5](https://www.kia.gov.tw/images/map_inl_arr/GoIn_map_b_05.jpg)![Driving route to International Arrival 6](https://www.kia.gov.tw/images/map_inl_arr/GoIn_map_b_06.jpg)![Driving route to International Arrival 7](https://www.kia.gov.tw/images/map_inl_arr/GoIn_map_b_07.jpg)![Driving route to International Arrival 8](https://www.kia.gov.tw/images/map_inl_arr/GoIn_map_b_08.jpg)![Driving route to International Arrival 9](https://www.kia.gov.tw/images/map_inl_arr/GoIn_map_b_09.jpg)\n\n#### To Int'l Terminal Arrival:\n\n1. Go straight along Zhongshan 4th Road.\n2. Slow down at curves.\n3. Turn left into the Kaohsiung Airport main entrance.\n4. Follow \"International Terminal\" signs and drive slowly to reach Arrival.\n\n#### Leaving for Downtown:\n\n1. Follow the signs outside the terminal.\n2. Pass under the viaduct and follow signs to Zhongshan 4th Road towards downtown.\n\n## Kaohsiung MRT (Metro)\n\n- The airport is served by **Kaohsiung International Airport Station (R4)** on the Red Line. Exit 2 connects to the Domestic Terminal; Exit 6 connects to the International Terminal. Four other exits are located within the parking area.\n- **Frequency:** 4-6 minutes during peak hours; no more than 8 minutes during off-peak hours.\n- To transfer to the Orange Line, please transfer at **Formosa Boulevard Station (O5/R10)**.\n\n[MRT Real-time Info](https://www.krtc.com.tw/Eng/Guide/station_info?id=0a444bb0f2eb4d0a88f451bdaf585fcc)\n\n## Intercity Buses\n\n### Southbound - To Kenting, Fangliao (via Hwy 17)\n\n- 9117 \\[Kaohsiung - Hwy 17 - Kenting\\]\n- 9117A \\[Kaohsiung - Hwy 17 - Fangliao\\]\n- 9117B Kenting Airport Express \\[Kaohsiung Zili Stn. - Airport - Dongliu Ferry - Kenting\\]\n\n#### Boarding Information\n\n- ✔ EasyCard / iPASS / TPASS accepted\n- ✔ Ticket Counter: Pingtung Bus Counter in the International Terminal Arrival Hall (1F), near Gate 5\n- ✔ Online Reservation: [Pingtung Travel Agency Official Website](https://maxa.chanjui.com/)\n\n**【Safety Notice: Do not take illegal/unlicensed taxis】**\n\n[9117 Route Map & Info](https://www.kbus.com.tw/bus_info.asp?id=165)\n\n### Northbound - To Tainan City\n\n- 111\n- (Service resumed starting August 2024)\n\n**【Safety Notice: Do not take illegal/unlicensed taxis】**\n\n[111 Route Map & Schedule](https://www.ibus.com.tw/city-bus/tnn/route111/)\n\n## Taxis\n\n### On-site Queued Taxis\n\n![Digital payment options for taxis](https://www.kia.gov.tw/images/trans/taxi/credit.jpg)\n\nInstant service available. Fares are metered (International Terminal pickups include a **NT$50 surcharge**; no surcharge for Domestic Terminal).\n\n**Waiting Area:** Curbside at the Arrival Exit (1F) of both Domestic and International Terminals.\n\n**Email:** [khhtaxi@gmail.com](mailto:khhtaxi@gmail.com)\n\nMultiple digital payment methods available starting Dec 15, 2024.\n\n#### Family-Friendly Taxi Service\n\n![Child safety seat taxi service](https://www.kia.gov.tw/images/trans/taxi/baby.jpg)\n\nCertified child safety seats available (Reservation required).\n\nPlease contact the Self-Discipline Committee for inquiries.\n\n**Phone:** (07)8071341\n\n**Email:** [khhtaxi@gmail.com](mailto:khhtaxi@gmail.com)\n\n### Important Notice\n\n![Airport taxi appearance](https://www.kia.gov.tw/images/trans/taxi/taxi.jpg)\n\nAirport taxis look like the image shown. To ensure service quality, if you encounter any violations, please record the license plate/queue number, driver's name, time, and location. Submit evidence to the [Director's Mailbox](https://kol.kia.gov.tw/zh_tw/feedback/mailbox) or call the Aviation Police (07-8011447).\n\n#### Violations include:\n\n1. Refusing passengers.\n2. Dirty vehicle or trunk filled with personal items.\n3. Detouring or overcharging (excluding the Int'l Terminal surcharge).\n4. Poor attitude or complaining about short trips.\n5. Forcing passengers off or switching cars halfway.\n6. Selling goods in the car.\n7. Other violations of the [Regulations for Civil Airport Passenger Transport](https://law.moj.gov.tw/LawClass/LawAll.aspx?pcode=K0040024).\n\n### International Terminal Pickup Locations\n\n![Map of taxi pickup locations](https://www.kia.gov.tw/images/trans/taxi/taximap.jpg)\n\n**Queued Taxis (Effective Nov 28, 2023):** Turn **left** after exiting the International Terminal Arrival Hall (outside FamilyMart at Gate 1). For Domestic Terminal, turn right after exiting (at Gate 3).\n\n**Patrol Taxis (Effective Nov 28, 2023):** Turn **right** after exiting the International Terminal Arrival Hall, near Pillar No. 9.\n\n**Rental Car Pick-up Point (Effective March 12, 2026):** Located at International Terminal Automatic Doors No. 8-9; passengers must wait for vehicles, and vehicles are not allowed to stay and wait for passengers. Additionally, 6 priority pick-up spaces for rental cars are provided in the Large Vehicle Parking Lot.\n\n#### Pre-Booked / Ride-Hailing Taxi Pick-Up Point\n\n**Effective June 25, 2026**, a new designated pick-up point for pre-booked taxis has been added as part of KHH's pilot program.\n\n\n![Pre-booked taxi pick-up point location map: proceed to the area behind MRT Exit 2, between the Domestic and International Terminals](https://www.kia.gov.tw/images/trans/taxi/%E7%B6%B2%E7%B4%84%E8%A8%88%E7%A8%8B%E8%BB%8A4.jpg)![Guidelines for pre-booked taxis: wait for vehicle at designated area, board as directed by staff, present booking confirmation on app](https://www.kia.gov.tw/images/trans/taxi/%E7%B6%B2%E7%B4%84%E8%A8%88%E7%A8%8B%E8%BB%8A1.jpg)![Participating taxi fleets: Zhonghua, Taiwan Taxi, Yoxi, Uber, LINE GO and others. Do not use unlicensed vehicles.](https://www.kia.gov.tw/images/trans/taxi/%E7%B6%B2%E7%B4%84%E8%A8%88%E7%A8%8B%E8%BB%8A3.jpg)\n\n## Parking\n\n[Check Availability & Reservations](https://airport.altob.com.tw/)\n\n![Airport Parking Layout Map](https://www.kia.gov.tw/images/trans/parking/parkinglayout.jpg)\n\nParking lots often fill up during holidays. Public transport is recommended. For long-term parking, please use off-site shuttle parking nearby:\n\n1. Dacheng (Dapeng) Parking: No. 207, Cuiheng South Road.\n2. Jinshan Parking: No. 58, Lane 133, Minyi Road.\n\n### Car Parking Map\n\nThere are 3 car parking lots: Opposite the Domestic Terminal, Opposite the International Terminal, and to the Right of the International Terminal. Follow the signs upon entering the airport roads.\n\n![Car parking route 1](https://www.kia.gov.tw/images/trans/parking/parkingroute1.jpg)![Car parking route 2](https://www.kia.gov.tw/images/trans/parking/parkingroute2.jpg)![Car parking route 3](https://www.kia.gov.tw/images/trans/parking/parkingroute3.jpg)\n\n### Motorcycle Parking\n\nThere are 2 motorcycle parking lots: Domestic Temporary Lot (Right side of Domestic Terminal) and International Lot (Left side of International Terminal). Follow the signs.\n\n#### International Motorcycle Parking Route\n\n![International motorcycle parking route](https://www.kia.gov.tw/images/trans/parking/motoparkingroute1.jpg)\n\n#### Domestic Temporary Motorcycle Parking\n\nExpected to open on **April 25**. Traffic flow has been adjusted; please drive carefully.\n\n![Domestic temporary motorcycle parking route](https://www.kia.gov.tw/images/trans/parking/motoparkingroute2.jpg)\n\n### Parking Rates\n\n| Fee Standard | Small Vehicle (Car) | Large Vehicle | Motorcycle |\n| --- | --- | --- | --- |\n| First hour (1st 30 mins) | Free | NT$50<br>(Free for rental shuttles) | - |\n| First hour (2nd 30 mins) | NT$30 | NT$50 | NT$20 per entry/day |\n| After 1st hour (Per 30 mins) | NT$15 | NT$25 | NT$20 per entry/day |\n| Daily Maximum | NT$240 | NT$475 | NT$20 per entry/day |\n| Monthly Pass (Non-stationed) | NT$3000 | NT$5000 | - |\n| Monthly Pass (Bus Operators) | NT$3000 | NT$3000 | - |\n| Staff Pass (Lots 1 & 3) | NT$600 | NT$1200 | NT$100 (Official) |\n| Staff Pass (Bus Lot) | NT$1200 | NT$1200 | NT$200 (Non-Official) |\n\nAirport Parking Fee Standards\n\n#### Remarks:\n\n- \"Daily\" calculation resets at 00:00 midnight.\n- Non-stationed units refer to:\n1. Licensed parking operators.\n2. Bus operators with counters in the airport.\n3. Contractors or delivery vendors.\n- Parking lots are open 24 hours.\n\n## Car Rental\n\n![Car Rental Center Exterior](https://www.kia.gov.tw/images/trans/rent/rentalphoto.jpg)\n\nThe Car Rental Center is located between the International and Domestic Terminals, near the bus waiting area.\n\n### How to get there?\n\n![Route to Car Rental Center](https://www.kia.gov.tw/images/trans/rent/carrent.jpg)\n\nFrom the Arrival Hall: Turn left and walk out of the lobby. Walk straight past the bus waiting area, then turn left. Walk straight for a distance, then turn right to reach the Rental Center.\n\n### Car Rental Contacts\n\n| Company Name | Location | Phone | Email |\n| --- | --- | --- | --- |\n| Hotai Leasing (iRent) | Rental Center | 07-8070333<br>07-8036060 | [khhstation@hotaimotor.com.tw](mailto:khhstation@hotaimotor.com.tw) |\n| Carplus Auto Leasing | Rental Center | 07-8010019 | [khh@car-plus.com.tw](mailto:khh@car-plus.com.tw) |\n| Chailease Auto Rental | Rental Center | 07-8018686 | [lyr01@chailease.com.tw](mailto:lyr01@chailease.com.tw) |\n\nCar Rental Company Contact Information\n\n## Bicycles\n\n### Kaohsiung Public Bike (YouBike)\n\n[Kaohsiung Public Bike (YouBike)](https://www.youbike.com.tw/region/kcg/en/). The \"Kaohsiung Park Station\" is located across from the airport entrance on Zhongshan 4th Road (with 8 bikes available). Access via the pedestrian crossing near the Domestic Motorcycle Parking entrance.\n\n### Bringing Bicycles on Flights\n\n**International:** Please check luggage regulations with your airline.\n\n**Domestic:** [UNI Air](https://www.uniair.com.tw/rwd/CMS/service/luggage_information), [Mandarin Airlines](https://www.mandarin-airlines.com/Content/%E5%B8%B8%E8%A6%8B%E5%95%8F%E9%A1%8C(QA)-2016100005-77)\n\n### Bicycle Storage\n\nPlease contact the Phoenix Baggage Counter (Int'l Terminal 3F): 07-8063366\n\n### Bicycles on MRT\n\n[KRTC Regulations for bringing regular and folding bicycles on the Metro.](https://www.krtc.com.tw/Eng/Guide/regulations?id=caf3666fcddc47b1a9ccf224c6ab677a)\n\n### Bicycles on Buses\n\nPlease contact the bus operators:\n\n- [0918 Kenting Express](https://www.ptbus.com.tw/news_info/0/1/118?status=5) 07-8032123\n- 9117/9117A Kaohsiung-Kenting Line: 0800-777-767\n- [111 Tainan-Airport Express](https://www.ibus.com.tw/city-bus/tnn/route111/) 0800-889-116\n\n### Cycling Route Information\n\nFor round-island cycling route information, please visit the Tourism Administration Travel Service Center at the airport. Phone: 07-8057888\n\n## About cookies\n\nThis site uses Google Analytics cookies to understand usage and improve our service. You may accept or decline; **declining does not affect any site function** — we will still measure overall traffic without identifying you.\n\nAcceptDecline [Privacy Policy](https://www.kia.gov.tw/EN/privacy-policy.html)",

        "summary": "The Kaohsiung International Airport provides key information about its facilities for travelers. The airport is located at No. 2, Zhongshan 4th Rd., Xiaogang Dist., Kaohsiung City. It features international and domestic terminals with various services including shops, food areas, and transportation options. Key amenities include ATMs, duty-free shops, car rental services, and ground transportation to downtown Kaohsiung via MRT and intercity buses. Safety notices about taxis and guidelines for bicycle handling are also provided. Parking options with rates and locations are detailed, emphasizing the importance of public transport during busy times."
