        "title": "How to Use Public Transportation",
        "description": "# Kaohsiung Transportation Guide\n## 🚇 Kaohsiung MRT\n- Ticket Purchase: Ticket vending machines and service counters are available at each station for single-journey or day passes.\n\n## 💳 E-Ticket Overview\n_Click Num:8891_\n\n_Share_\n\nLoading...",
        "position": 9,
        "markdown": "[Jump to the main content block](https://employment.nsysu.edu.tw/p/404-1374-353092.php?Lang=en#start-C \"Jump to the main content block\")\n\n1. [Home](https://employment.nsysu.edu.tw/index.php)\n\n## How to Use Public Transportation\n\n# Kaohsiung Transportation Guide\n\n## 🚇 Kaohsiung MRT\n\n- Ticket Purchase: Ticket vending machines and service counters are available at each station for single-journey or day passes.\n- Entering/Exiting Gates: Tap your card to enter. For single-journey tickets, insert it at the gate when exiting.\n- Riding Rules: Smoking, eating, and chewing gum are prohibited. Please keep the carriage clean and quiet.\n- Route Info: The MRT currently includes the Red and Orange lines, which connect to major landmarks and stations.\n\n## 🚈 Kaohsiung Light Rail\n\n- How to Ride: Tap your e-ticket card on the platform both when entering and exiting.\n- Ticket Usage: iPASS, EasyCard, and other e-tickets are accepted; fares are the same as MRT.\n- Route Highlights: The circular route connects MRT stations, Pier-2 Art Center, Dream Mall, and other hot spots.\n- Reminders: The light rail runs at street level, so please mind traffic and follow station instructions when boarding or alighting.\n\n## 🚌 Bus Riding Tips\n\n- Please use e-tickets (e.g., EasyCard, iPASS, iCash). You can pay NT$12 in cash, but no change is given.\n- Most bus stops in Kaohsiung have smart signs or dynamic displays, and you can check schedules via apps or QR codes.\n- Bus stops display route info, departure times, and contact numbers—you can call to check for the last bus.\n- Remember to wave at the bus to signal it to stop.\n- Before boarding, check the bus number and destination—major stops are marked on the bus exterior.\n- Boarding: Tap your card when getting on and off. You don’t need to check the \"Pay on Boarding/Alighting\" light.\n- Facilities: Coin boxes are near the front door; card readers are near both front and back doors. Prepare in advance.\n- Announcement System: Stops are shown via LED signs and announced in multiple languages.\n- Alighting: Press the stop button before your stop and take all belongings with you.\n- Etiquette: Yield priority seats. Smoking, eating, and drinking are prohibited on the bus.\n\n## 🚴‍♀️ Public Bicycles (YouBike)\n\n- Kaohsiung uses \"YouBike 2.0\", available 24/7 for rental.\n- Use iPASS, EasyCard, or the official YouBike App (linked to your credit card) to scan and unlock.\n- Stations are located near MRT stations, universities, business areas, and parks for easy access.\n- After use, return the bike by pushing it into the dock and confirming with a card tap or via the app.\n- The first 30 minutes are charged at a lower rate. Billing is based on time; promotions vary by official announcements.\n- Check real-time bike and dock availability via the app or Transportation Bureau website.\n\n## 💳 E-Ticket Overview\n\n- **[iPASS](https://www.i-pass.com.tw/)**: Usable across Taiwan for transportation and purchases, with reward point accumulation.\n- **[EasyCard](https://www.easycard.com.tw/)**: Supports Taipei MRT, buses, parking, and partner stores.\n- Both support balance check, auto top-up, and promotional offers via apps.\n\n_Click Num:8891_\n\n_Share_\n\n[![Facebook.](https://employment.nsysu.edu.tw/static/file/374/1374/stsns/12.png)](https://www.facebook.com/profile.php?id=61577115076699 \"facebook.\")[![Instagram](https://employment.nsysu.edu.tw/static/file/374/1374/stsns/11.png)](https://www.instagram.com/nsysu_employmenthub/ \"instagram\")\n\nClose (Esc)ShareToggle fullscreenZoom in/out\n\nPrevious (arrow left)Next (arrow right)\n\nLoading...",

        "summary": "The Kaohsiung Transportation Guide provides essential information on various public transport options available in Kaohsiung, Taiwan, including the MRT, Light Rail, buses, and public bicycles (YouBike). Key points include: 1. **MRT**: Ticket purchasing options, entry/exit procedures, riding rules, and route information for the Red and Orange lines. 2. **Light Rail**: E-ticket usage instructions, route highlights, and safety reminders. 3. **Buses**: Payment options (e-tickets preferred), features at bus stops, boarding procedures, and etiquette. 4. **YouBike**: Rental procedures, payment methods, and bike availability. 5. **E-Tickets**: Overview of iPASS and EasyCard, including their usage and benefits for transportation and purchases."

      {
        "url": "https://goldcard.nat.gov.tw/en/why-taiwan/public-transportation-getting-around-taiwan/",
        "title": "Public Transportation - Getting Around Taiwan - Employment Gold Card",
        "description": "# Public Transportation - Getting Around Taiwan\n## Mass Rapid Transit (MRT)\nIt also connects the Zuoying Station in the north to the Kaohsiung International Airport in the south.\n\n- [Kaohsiung Metro Info](https://www.krtc.com.tw/)",
        "position": 10,
        "markdown": "TGC will be closed on October 23 due to the typhoon, and on October 26 in observance of Taiwan Retrocession Day.\n\nCloseClose\n\n![](https://goldcard.nat.gov.tw/cms-uploads/public-transportation-getting-around-taiwan.jpg)\n\n# Public Transportation - Getting Around Taiwan\n\nTaiwan is known for its amazing public transportation system, garnering praises from both locals and tourists for how safe, clean, and reliable it is. You can even travel to major cities and attractions by different types of public transport. The following is a basic introduction to Taiwan’s various types of public transportation.\n\n## Taiwan High-Speed Rail (HSR)\n\nSince its official opening in 2007, the High-Speed Rail has become the fastest means of transportation in Taiwan, with stations located in 11 major cities, including Taipei, Taoyuan, Hsinchu, Taichung, Tainan and Kaohsiung. Reaching high speeds of 300 kilometers per hour, it only takes 95 minutes to travel between Taipei and Kaohsiung.\nTourists can wake up early, take the HSR from Taipei and arrive in Kaohsiung before noon, then take the Kaohsiung MRT and ferry to Qijin Fishing Port to enjoy some fresh seafood. If there’s time, on the way back through Taichung take a stroll through the famous Feng Chia Night Market for some more tasty delicacies. After eating and drinking your fill, you are ready to head home with a full belly and great memories.\n\n[![Taiwan High-Speed Rail (HSR)](https://goldcard.nat.gov.tw/cms-uploads/taiwan-highspeedrail-700t-testrun-2006-0624.jpg)](https://goldcard.nat.gov.tw/cms-uploads/taiwan-highspeedrail-700t-testrun-2006-0624.jpg \"Taiwan High-Speed Rail (HSR)\")\n\nEncino from zhwiki, Public domain, via Wikimedia Commons\n\nClose\n\n![Taiwan High-Speed Rail (HSR)](https://goldcard.nat.gov.tw/cms-uploads/taiwan-highspeedrail-700t-testrun-2006-0624.jpg)\n\n- [Ticketing Info](https://en.thsrc.com.tw/ \"Opens a new window: to Taiwan High-Speed Rail Website\")\n\n## Taiwan Railway\n\nThe long history of the Taiwan Railway is a common memory for many Taiwanese people. Though it may be slower than the HSR, the Taiwan Railway is an island-wide network that connects different cities and counties in the western and eastern regions of Taiwan. With 241 stations and relatively inexpensive fares, it remains one of the most convenient and popular forms of public transportation, especially during the holidays when many locals need to return home.\n\nThe Taiwan Railways’ train ride around the island is a very popular attraction. Tourists can witness the splendid views of Taiwan’s cities and admire the beautiful scenery of its countryside, all from the comfort of their well-designed and spacious train. The most charming part of the ride is the East Rift Valley (also known as the Huadong Valley). During the Spring, tourists are treated to a sea of wild flowers scattered across the mountainside; in the Autumn during harvest season, they can witness an abundance of wheat ears, glistening under the sun. Regardless of what season it is, the thriving and vibrant life of Taiwan’s countryside is apparent. For tourists who wish to travel on the Taiwan Railway during long holidays, especially the Eastern Route trains, please remember to book your tickets in advance as tickets sell out fast!\n\n[![Taiwan Railway](https://goldcard.nat.gov.tw/cms-uploads/taiwan-railway.jpg)](https://goldcard.nat.gov.tw/cms-uploads/taiwan-railway.jpg \"Taiwan Railway\")\n\nClose\n\n![Taiwan Railway](https://goldcard.nat.gov.tw/cms-uploads/taiwan-railway.jpg)\n\n- [Ticketing Info](https://www.railway.gov.tw/tra-tip-web/tip \"Opens a new window:  to Taiwan Railway Website\")\n\n## Charter Bus System\n\nTaiwan’s Charter Bus System is also very well-developed, with prices cheaper than the Taiwan Railway. However, unlike the Taiwan Railway, aside from a few major metropolitan areas, most stops are located near highway interchanges, far from the city. Therefore, if you decide to take a charter bus, it is likely that you will need to arrange for another ride later.\n\nCurrently, there are many charter bus companies in Taiwan, such as Kuo-Kuang eBus, United Highway Bus (Ubus), Ho-Hsin eBus, Kamalan Bus, Hsinchu Bus, etc. It may be daunting at first for foreigners to determine which bus company best fits their need, but just remember that aside from Kuo-Kuang eBus, each bus company has specific cities in which they operate, so the easiest way to decide is to choose from the departure and arrival cities.\nIn order to promote tourism and remedy the insufficiencies of the existing highway charter bus system, the Tourism Bureau of the Ministry of Transportation and Communications of Taiwan launched the Taiwan Tourist Shuttle bus service in 2010 to shuttle passengers to and from nearby major Taiwan Railway and HSR stations. As long as you time it right, you can almost always catch a ride, making it one of the most popular means of transportation for backpackers.\n\n[![Charter Bus System](https://goldcard.nat.gov.tw/cms-uploads/charter-bus-system.jpg)](https://goldcard.nat.gov.tw/cms-uploads/charter-bus-system.jpg \"Charter Bus System\")\n\nFrom Tourism Bureau\n\nClose\n\n![Charter Bus System](https://goldcard.nat.gov.tw/cms-uploads/charter-bus-system.jpg)\n\n- [List of Charter Bus Companies](https://zh.wikipedia.org/wiki/%E4%B8%AD%E8%8F%AF%E6%B0%91%E5%9C%8B%E6%B1%BD%E8%BB%8A%E5%AE%A2%E9%81%8B%E6%A5%AD%E8%80%85%E5%88%97%E8%A1%A8 \"Opens a new window:  to List of Charter Bus Companies Website\")\n- [Taiwan Tourist Shuttle](https://www.taiwantrip.com.tw/ \"Opens a new window:  to Taiwan Trip Website\")\n\n## Mass Rapid Transit (MRT)\n\nBright and clean MRT cars have become the new standard for modern cities. There are currently five MRT systems in Taiwan: Taipei, Kaohsiung, Taoyuan, New Taipei and Taichung, listed in order of their establishment. If the MRT is your main mode of transportation, it is more cost-effective to purchase a monthly pass. In addition, to ensure the comfort of passengers and keep the MRT cars clean, no food or drink is allowed in the station or on the trains.\nThe Taipei Mass Rapid Transit, branded as the Taipei Metro, was the first MRT to be established in Taiwan, and has since developed six different lines covering almost the entire Taipei metropolitan area, allowing many landmarks and popular attractions to be accessible by MRT. The furthest north you can travel by MRT is Danshui, the south is Xindian Bitan, the west to Xinzhuang or Tucheng, and the east to Nangang, where the first professional large-scale, double-story exhibition hall in Taiwan is located - the go-to venue for many major international exhibitions. With such a well-connected network and trains that almost are never delayed, the Taipei Metro definitely ranks as one of the top metro systems internationally.\n\nThe Kaohsiung MRT was officially opened in 2008, and currently has three main lines: the Red Line, the Orange Line, and the Circular Light Rail Line. It also connects the Zuoying Station in the north to the Kaohsiung International Airport in the south. Well-known stations include Formosa Boulevard, Weiwuying, Central Park, and Sizihwan. Formosa Boulevard station is renowned for its beautiful “Dome of Light” stained glass art display, the world’s largest public art installation made from pieces of colored glass. In addition to the 3 lines mentioned, the Kaohsiung MRT continues to propose plans to increase the number of lines and stations, moving towards the ideal of a 30-minute living circle in Greater Kaohsiung.The Taoyuan MRT is the third MRT system in operation, with network that covers three cities: Taipei City, New Taipei City and Taoyuan City. The Airport MRT is connected to the Taipei Metro, making it easy for inbound and outbound passengers to get to Taipei in about 30 minutes by direct train.The New Taipei and Taichung MRT systems were opened later. The New Taipei MRT is mainly operated by the\n\nTaipei Metro Corporation, and consists of the Danhai Light Rail, Ankeng Light Rail, and Sanying Line. Currently, only the Danhai Light Rail is in operation. The Taichung MRT consists of the Green, Blue, and Orange Line, with only the Green Line (Wurih-Wunsin-Beitun line) currently being in operation, and connects the Taichung HSR station with City Hall.\n\n[![Mass Rapid Transit (MRT)](https://goldcard.nat.gov.tw/cms-uploads/mrt.jpg)](https://goldcard.nat.gov.tw/cms-uploads/mrt.jpg \"Mass Rapid Transit (MRT)\")\n\nClose\n\n![Mass Rapid Transit (MRT)](https://goldcard.nat.gov.tw/cms-uploads/mrt.jpg)\n\n- [Taipei Metro Info](https://english.metro.taipei/ \"Opens a new window:  to Taipei Metro Website\")\n- [Taichung Metro Info](https://www.tmrt.com.tw/ \"Opens a new window:  to Taichung Metro Website\")\n- [Kaohsiung Metro Info](https://www.krtc.com.tw/ \"Opens a new window:  to Kaohsiung Metro Website\")\n- [New Taipei City Metro Info](https://www.ntmetro.com.tw/ \"Opens a new window:  to New Taipei City Metro Website\")\n- [Taoyuan Airport Metro Info](https://www.taoyuan-airport.com/ \"Opens a new window:  to Taoyuan Airport Metro Website\")\n\n## City Bus\n\nCity buses are the most common yet indispensable means of public transportation in all cities. Many people choose city buses as their main means of transportation because of their frequency, convenience, and number of stops.\n\nAll counties and cities in Taiwan have a city bus system, with Taipei, Taichung, Tainan and Kaohsiung having the most developed and concentrated bus routes. In order to encourage the public to take the bus more often, each county and city offers special discount programs from time to time, such as Taichung’s Double 10 Bus Fare Scheme, and the Taipei Fun Pass, so you can travel the entire city without having to drive yourself.\n\n**Your EasyCard is accepted on the city bus so you don’t need to pay with change.**\n\n[![City Bus](https://goldcard.nat.gov.tw/cms-uploads/bus.jpg)](https://goldcard.nat.gov.tw/cms-uploads/bus.jpg \"City Bus\")\n\nClose\n\n![City Bus](https://goldcard.nat.gov.tw/cms-uploads/bus.jpg)\n\n- [Taipei City Bus Info](https://ebus.gov.taipei/ \"Opens a new window: to Taipei City Bus Website\")\n- [Taichung City Bus Info](https://citybus.taichung.gov.tw/ebus \"Opens a new window: to Taichung City Bus Website\")\n- [Tainan City Bus Info](https://2384.tainan.gov.tw/newtnbusweb/ebusInfo.html? \"Opens a new window: to Tainan City Bus Website\")\n- [Kaohsiung City Bus Info](https://ibus.tbkc.gov.tw/ibus/ \"Opens a new window: to Kaohsiung City Bus Website\")\n\n## Public Bicycles (You-Bike/E-Bike/City Bike/ iBike/ T-Bike)\n\nPublic bicycles have frequently been seen as a way to solve the “last mile” problem, and connect users between public transit networks and their final destination. Since 2008, Taipei and other major cities have been promoting public bicycles and setting up more docking stations to conserve energy, reduce the city’s carbon footprint, and encourage a healthy lifestyle with exercise.\n\nDue to the increase of cyclists, the city has gradually begun to change its traffic system to ensure cyclists’ safety and right-of-way. For example, Taipei City has reduced car lanes just to make room for more bike lanes.\nCurrently, most of the major public bicycles in the country are manufactured by Giant, Taiwan’s number one bicycle brand. Some regions even entrust Giant to operate their bicycles. The design of the bike is very user-friendly, complete with wide seats and 3-speed gear shift. The ability to pay with an EasyCard (or other similar cards), makes it very convenient and highly praised.\n\n[![Public Bicycles](https://goldcard.nat.gov.tw/cms-uploads/youbike_station_in_changhua_01.jpg)](https://goldcard.nat.gov.tw/cms-uploads/youbike_station_in_changhua_01.jpg \"Public Bicycles\")\n\nLtdccba, CC BY-SA 4.0 [https://creativecommons.org/licenses/by-sa/4.0](https://creativecommons.org/licenses/by-sa/4.0), via Wikimedia Commons\n\nClose\n\n![Public Bicycles](https://goldcard.nat.gov.tw/cms-uploads/youbike_station_in_changhua_01.jpg)\n\n- [Public Bicycle Station App](https://play.google.com/store/apps/details?id=tw.com.youbike.plus&hl=en \"Opens a new window:  to Public Bicycle Station App Website\")\n\n## Taxi\n\nTaxis in Taiwan are mostly yellow in color, hence their nickname, “Xiao Huang”, which directly translates to Little Yellow. There are many ways to hail a taxi, such as phoning for one, using an app, or just stopping one on the street. In addition to the common taxi companies such as Taiwan Taxi and Metro Taxi, in recent years, technological innovation and advancements have led to the rise of new era taxis, such as Uber, Line Taxi, and Yoxi, diversifying the options for riders. Taxi companies will also offer special discount fares from time to time, and riders can pay by cash or credit, something that is convenient for both locals and foreigners.\n\n[![Taxi](https://goldcard.nat.gov.tw/cms-uploads/taxi.jpg)](https://goldcard.nat.gov.tw/cms-uploads/taxi.jpg \"Taxi\")\n\nClose\n\n![Taxi](https://goldcard.nat.gov.tw/cms-uploads/taxi.jpg)\n\nGo to the top\n\nClose\n\n[en](https://goldcard.nat.gov.tw/en/why-taiwan/public-transportation-getting-around-taiwan/ \"Change language to English\") [zh](https://goldcard.nat.gov.tw/en/why-taiwan/public-transportation-getting-around-taiwan/ \"變更語言至中文\") [ja](https://goldcard.nat.gov.tw/en/why-taiwan/public-transportation-getting-around-taiwan/ \"言語を日本語に変更します\")\n\nClose\n\nSearchReset filterReset filter\n\nReset filterMinimum of 3 characters\n\nReset filterMinimum of 3 characters\n\nReset filterNo results\n\n![Whasky](<Base64-Image-Removed>)Open AssistantOpen Assistant",

        "summary": "TGC will be closed on October 23 due to a typhoon and on October 26 for Taiwan Retrocession Day. Taiwan's public transportation options include:\n\n1. **High-Speed Rail (HSR)**: Fastest travel option since 2007, connecting major cities with speeds up to 300 km/h.\n2. **Taiwan Railway**: Extensive and historical network, popular for scenic rides around the island. Tickets should be booked in advance during holidays.\n3. **Charter Bus System**: Affordable but less accessible than rail services; provides connections to tourist attractions.\n4. **Mass Rapid Transit (MRT)**: Clean and efficient metro systems across several cities, with the Taipei Metro being the most notable.\n5. **City Buses**: Frequent and convenient, with EasyCard payment options.\n6. **Public Bicycles**: Promote sustainable transport, with numerous docking stations and easy payment methods.\n7. **Taxis**: Commonly yellow, available via phone or apps, with various providers including Uber."

Transportation: Plan Your Route and Save\nGetting around Kaohsiung is both straightforward and affordable, making it easy to explore the city on any travel budget.\n\nFor airport arrivals, the **Kaohsiung MRT** provides a convenient connection from Kaohsiung International Airport to the city centre.\n\n## How Much to Bring to Kaohsiung: Budget Guidelines\n| Category | Budget (NT$) | Mid-range (NT$) | Comfortable (NT$) |\n|-|-|-|-|\n| Transportation | NT$100–250 | NT$300–600 | NT$700–1,200 |\n| Food & Drinks | NT$300–600 | NT$700–1,200 | NT$1,500–2,500 |\n| Attractions | NT$0–300 | NT$400–800 | NT$1,000–2,000 |\n| **Total / Day** | **NT$400–1,150** | **NT$1,400–2,600** | **NT$3,200–5,700+** |\n\nBudget travellers can expect to spend around **NT$400–1,150 per day** by using public transport, eating at local restaurants and night markets, and visiting mostly free or low-cost attractions. A mid-range budget of **NT$1,400–2,600 per day** allows for more comfortable transport, restaurant meals, and several paid attractions. For a more comfortable experience with taxis, nicer dining, and premium activities, daily spending may reach **NT$3,200–5,700+**.\n\n| Category | Budget (NT$) | Mid-range (NT$) | Comfortable (NT$) |\n|-|-|-|-|\n| Transportation | NT$100–250 | NT$300–600 | NT$700–1,200 |\n| Food & Drinks | NT$300–600 | NT$700–1,200 | NT$1,500–2,500 |\n| Attractions | NT$0–300 | NT$400–800 | NT$1,000–2,000 |\n| **Total / Day** | **NT$400–1,150** | **NT$1,400–2,600** | **NT$3,200–5,700+** |\n\nBudget travellers can expect to spend around **NT$400–1,150 per day** by using public transport, eating at local restaurants and night markets, and visiting mostly free or low-cost attractions.         "markdown": "![](https://www.airasia.com/cdn-cgi/image/width=1920,quality=75,format=auto/https://rad.airasia.com/assets/f447b40d-8f2e-4a51-bc13-fdf05f3372ad)\n\n![How Much to Bring to Kaohsiung and How to Pay](https://www.airasia.com/cdn-cgi/image/width=1920,quality=75,format=auto/https://rad.airasia.com/assets/f447b40d-8f2e-4a51-bc13-fdf05f3372ad)\n\n# How Much to Bring to Kaohsiung and How to Pay\n\nKaohsiung Expenses, Travel Costs, and Payment Options\n\nPlanning a trip to Kaohsiung but not sure how much money to bring? Kaohsiung attracts travellers with its vibrant night markets, delicious Taiwanese food, scenic waterfront, temples and laid-back atmosphere. But is Kaohsiung expensive to visit? The answer depends on how you travel. With affordable local food, convenient public transport and plenty of low-cost attractions, you can enjoy Kaohsiung without overspending.\n\nWhether you’re a backpacker looking for affordable street food and local experiences or a comfort-seeker planning a relaxing city break, understanding your daily expenses is key. In this guide, we’ll break down how much money to bring to Kaohsiung, from food and transportation to attractions and everyday spending, with practical budgets for different types of travellers.\n\n### Why Traveling to Kaohsiung Doesn’t Have to Be Expensive\n\nIs Kaohsiung expensive to visit when it comes to everyday cash needs? A key question for anyone visiting Kaohsiung is: when will you actually need cash? While hotels, major attractions, shopping malls and chain stores generally accept cards, everyday experiences can differ. You may need physical cash for **night market vendors, traditional food stalls, small local shops, traditional markets and some taxis**, where card payments may not always be available.\n\nYou may also want cash for spontaneous food purchases, small cafés, local markets and other everyday expenses. Therefore, keeping around **NT$1,000–2,000 in cash** can be a practical starting point, plus a small backup reserve for emergencies. For larger purchases, you can often use cards or contactless payment where accepted. Planning your cash needs in advance can help you avoid unnecessary ATM fees and enjoy Kaohsiung’s local food, markets and attractions with greater convenience.\n\n## How Much to Bring to Kaohsiung – Budget Guidelines\n\nYour daily expenses in Kaohsiung depend on your travel style.\n\nBudget travellers can keep costs low by choosing local food, using public transport and visiting free or low-cost attractions, while those looking for more comfort can enjoy nicer restaurants, paid attractions and quality accommodation at a higher but manageable cost.\n\nAs a general rule, carry an extra **NT$500–1,000 per day** as a buffer for unexpected expenses, such as additional transport, meals or last-minute activities.\n\n## Flights: Getting to Kaohsiung on a Budget\n\nFlights are often the largest expense when planning your trip. Round-trip flights from Malaysia to Kaohsiung typically range between **RM500 to RM1,500**, depending on the airline, booking time, and travel season.\n\n**Tips to save:**\n\n- Book 1–2 months in advance\n- Be flexible with travel dates; midweek flights may be cheaper\n- Use flight + hotel bundles via AirAsia MOVE for extra savings\n\nEven on a budget, you can fly to Kaohsiung without spending a fortune and have more money left for local food, attractions, shopping and other experiences.\n\n[![Kuala Lumpur (KUL) - Kaohsiung (KHH)](https://www.airasia.com/cdn-cgi/image/width=1920,quality=75,format=auto/https://rad.airasia.com/assets/b4de5aa9-7af0-4d24-9142-5bfd84453a2a)\\\\\n\\\\\n**Kuala Lumpur (KUL) - Kaohsiung (KHH)** \\\\\n\\\\\nCheap flights from Kuala Lumpur (KUL) - Kaohsiung (KHH)\\\\\n\\\\\nSTART FROMTWD 3,124 (MYR 391)\\\\\n\\\\\nBOOK FLIGHT](https://www.airasia.com/v2/flights/search/?origin=KUL&destination=KHH&departDate=02%2F10%2F2026&tripType=O&adult=1&child=0&infant=0&locale=en-gb&currency=MYR&airlineProfile=k%2Cd%2Cg&type=paired&cabinClass=economy&upsellWidget=true&upsellPremiumFlatbedWidget=true&isOC=true&isDC=false&uce=true&ancillaryAbTest=false&isAirasiaFlightOnly=false&providers=&taIDs=&j=cds)\n\n## Accommodation: Where to Stay\n\nKaohsiung offers accommodation options for every type of traveller. Budget travellers can stay in hostels or guesthouses, while mid-range travellers can choose comfortable hotels and boutique stays near popular attractions and transport links.\n\nPopular areas to stay include:\n\n\\\\* **Formosa Boulevard** – convenient for public transport, night markets, restaurants and exploring the city \\* **Yancheng District** – great for travellers interested in local food, cafés, cultural attractions and the waterfront \\* **Xinxing District** – a lively area with shopping, restaurants, nightlife and easy access to public transport \\* **Kaohsiung City Centre** – ideal for travellers who want convenient access to shopping, dining, attractions and major transport links\n\nChoosing accommodation close to an **MRT station** can save time and transportation costs, making it easier to explore different parts of Kaohsiung.\n\nBooking early through AirAsia MOVE can help secure better prices and convenient locations.\n\n[![Uns B&B](https://www.airasia.com/cdn-cgi/image/width=1920,quality=75,format=auto/https://rad.airasia.com/assets/1649f8f1-3772-4562-a24f-ba48167f8da4)\\\\\n\\\\\n**Uns B&B** \\\\\n\\\\\nNo.23-2, Kujiang Street, Kaohsiung, Taiwan, Province of China, 80344\\\\\n\\\\\nFrom TWD 1,949per night per person\\\\\n\\\\\nBook Now](https://www.airasia.com/hotel/kaohsiung/uns-b-and-b/18894?country_code=my&checkin=2027-02-19&checkout=2027-02-21&occupancy=2&location_id=1808&languageId=en-gb&currency=TWD)\n\n[![Kaohsiung International Plaza](https://www.airasia.com/cdn-cgi/image/width=1920,quality=75,format=auto/https://rad.airasia.com/assets/1291809e-fa4b-4a33-9883-5a6e107f992f)\\\\\n\\\\\n**Kaohsiung International Plaza** \\\\\n\\\\\nNo.436, Daye N. Rd., Siaogang District, Kaohsiung, Taiwan\\\\\n\\\\\nFrom TWD 1,894per night per person\\\\\n\\\\\nBook Now](https://www.airasia.com/hotel/kaohsiung/kaohsiung-international-plaza/3c4dc5?country_code=my&checkin=2027-02-19&checkout=2027-02-21&occupancy=2&location_id=1808&languageId=en-gb&currency=TWD)\n\n[![HUB HOTEL KAOHSIUNG YISIN BRANCH](https://www.airasia.com/cdn-cgi/image/width=1920,quality=75,format=auto/https://rad.airasia.com/assets/d19604ab-0c80-419e-8a5c-c96fabb03da6)\\\\\n\\\\\n**HUB HOTEL KAOHSIUNG YISIN BRANCH** \\\\\n\\\\\nNo. 15, Yixin 2nd Road, Kaohsiung, Taiwan\\\\\n\\\\\nFrom TWD 1,920per night per person\\\\\n\\\\\nBook Now](https://www.airasia.com/hotel/kaohsiung/hub-hotel-kaohsiung-yisin-branch/e4bc30?country_code=my&checkin=2027-02-19&checkout=2027-02-21&occupancy=2&location_id=1808&languageId=en-gb&currency=TWD)\n\n[![Hotel Nikko Kaohsiung](https://www.airasia.com/cdn-cgi/image/width=1920,quality=75,format=auto/https://rad.airasia.com/assets/e95cf57d-0119-498e-a9fc-55b182f1a432)\\\\\n\\\\\n**Hotel Nikko Kaohsiung** \\\\\n\\\\\nNo. 268, Linsen 4th Rd., Kaohsiung, Taiwan, Province of China\\\\\n\\\\\nFrom TWD 5,592per night per person\\\\\n\\\\\nBook Now](https://www.airasia.com/hotel/kaohsiung/hotel-nikko-kaohsiung/3d203ec?country_code=my&checkin=2027-02-19&checkout=2027-02-21&occupancy=2&location_id=1808&languageId=en-gb&currency=TWD)\n\nKaohsiung offers accommodation options for every type of traveller. Budget travellers can stay in hostels or guesthouses, while mid-range travellers can choose comfortable hotels and boutique stays near popular attractions and transport links.\n\nPopular areas to stay include:\n\n\\\\* **Formosa Boulevard** – convenient for public transport, night markets, restaurants and exploring the city \\* **Yancheng District** – great for travellers interested in local food, cafés, cultural attractions and the waterfront \\* **Xinxing District** – a lively area with shopping, restaurants, nightlife and easy access to public transport \\* **Kaohsiung City Centre** – ideal for travellers who want convenient access to shopping, dining, attractions and major transport links\n\nChoosing accommodation close to an **MRT station** can save time and transportation costs, making it easier to explore different parts of Kaohsiung.\n\nBooking early through AirAsia MOVE can help secure better prices and convenient locations.\n\n[![Uns B&B](https://www.airasia.com/cdn-cgi/image/width=1920,quality=75,format=auto/https://rad.airasia.com/assets/1649f8f1-3772-4562-a24f-ba48167f8da4)\\\\\n\\\\\n**Uns B&B** \\\\\n\\\\\nNo.23-2, Kujiang Street, Kaohsiung, Taiwan, Province of China, 80344\\\\\n\\\\\nFrom TWD 1,949per night per person\\\\\n\\\\\nBook Now](https://www.airasia.com/hotel/kaohsiung/uns-b-and-b/18894?country_code=my&checkin=2027-02-19&checkout=2027-02-21&occupancy=2&location_id=1808&languageId=en-gb&currency=TWD)\n\n[![Kaohsiung International Plaza](https://www.airasia.com/cdn-cgi/image/width=1920,quality=75,format=auto/https://rad.airasia.com/assets/1291809e-fa4b-4a33-9883-5a6e107f992f)\\\\\n\\\\\n**Kaohsiung International Plaza** \\\\\n\\\\\nNo.436, Daye N. Rd., Siaogang District, Kaohsiung, Taiwan\\\\\n\\\\\nFrom TWD 1,894per night per person\\\\\n\\\\\nBook Now](https://www.airasia.com/hotel/kaohsiung/kaohsiung-international-plaza/3c4dc5?country_code=my&checkin=2027-02-19&checkout=2027-02-21&occupancy=2&location_id=1808&languageId=en-gb&currency=TWD)\n\n[![HUB HOTEL KAOHSIUNG YISIN BRANCH](https://www.airasia.com/cdn-cgi/image/width=1920,quality=75,format=auto/https://rad.airasia.com/assets/d19604ab-0c80-419e-8a5c-c96fabb03da6)\\\\\n\\\\\n**HUB HOTEL KAOHSIUNG YISIN BRANCH** \\\\\n\\\\\nNo. 15, Yixin 2nd Road, Kaohsiung, Taiwan\\\\\n\\\\\nFrom TWD 1,920per night per person\\\\\n\\\\\nBook Now](https://www.airasia.com/hotel/kaohsiung/hub-hotel-kaohsiung-yisin-branch/e4bc30?country_code=my&checkin=2027-02-19&checkout=2027-02-21&occupancy=2&location_id=1808&languageId=en-gb&currency=TWD)\n\n[![Hotel Nikko Kaohsiung](https://www.airasia.com/cdn-cgi/image/width=1920,quality=75,format=auto/https://rad.airasia.com/assets/e95cf57d-0119-498e-a9fc-55b182f1a432)\\\\\n\\\\\n**Hotel Nikko Kaohsiung** \\\\\n\\\\\nNo. 268, Linsen 4th Rd., Kaohsiung, Taiwan, Province of China\\\\\n\\\\\nFrom TWD 5,592per night per person\\\\\n\\\\\nBook Now](https://www.airasia.com/hotel/kaohsiung/hotel-nikko-kaohsiung/3d203ec?country_code=my&checkin=2027-02-19&checkout=2027-02-21&occupancy=2&location_id=1808&languageId=en-gb&currency=TWD)\n\n## Transportation: Plan Your Route and Save\n\nGetting around Kaohsiung is both straightforward and affordable, making it easy to explore the city on any travel budget. Most locals and visitors use the **MRT and public buses**, while the light rail is another convenient option for reaching attractions around the waterfront and city centre. For example, MRT journeys between major areas are generally affordable, while public buses can help you reach local neighbourhoods and attractions without spending much.\n\nFor airport arrivals, the **Kaohsiung MRT** provides a convenient connection from Kaohsiung International Airport to the city centre. Sightseeing can be customized: splurge on experiences such as the **Love River, Fo Guang Shan Buddha Museum or boat rides around Lotus Pond**, or fill your days with free attractions, waterfront walks and local markets. Many popular experiences cost little or nothing, from exploring **Pier-2 Art Center** to wandering **Liuhe Night Market** and enjoying the city’s waterfront scenery.\n\nOverall, combining public transport with self-guided exploration offers a convenient and wallet-friendly way to experience Kaohsiung.\n\n## Attractions: Must-See Kaohsiung Sites\n\nKaohsiung offers a mix of free city attractions and affordable activities, making it easy to explore without overspending. Many of the city’s most memorable experiences, including waterfront views, night markets, parks, cultural sites and neighbourhoods, can be enjoyed for free or at a low cost.\n\n**Free Attractions in Kaohsiung**\n\n1\\. **Love River** – Take a relaxing walk along the riverfront, enjoy the city scenery and experience one of Kaohsiung’s most popular waterfront areas without an entrance fee.\n\n2\\. **Pier-2 Art Center** – Explore colourful public art, creative installations, warehouses and outdoor spaces in this popular arts and cultural district. Many outdoor areas can be visited for free.\n\n3\\. **Liuhe Night Market** – Wander through one of Kaohsiung’s best-known night markets and browse local street food, drinks, snacks and souvenirs without an entrance fee.\n\n4\\. **Lotus Pond** – Visit this scenic lake in Zuoying and see landmarks such as the Dragon and Tiger Pagodas while enjoying the surrounding temples and waterfront scenery.\n\n**Paid Attractions and Activities in Kaohsiung**\n\n1\\. **Fo Guang Shan Buddha Museum** – Visit one of Kaohsiung’s major cultural and Buddhist attractions, featuring impressive architecture, gardens and exhibitions. Entry policies and fees may vary by area or activity.\n\n2\\. **Kaohsiung Harbour Cruise** – Enjoy views of Kaohsiung’s waterfront, harbour and skyline from the water. Ticket prices vary depending on the cruise and package.\n\n3\\. **Kaohsiung Music Center** – Explore the waterfront complex and its cultural spaces, with ticket prices varying for specific exhibitions, performances or events.\n\n4\\. **Kaohsiung Zoo** – Visit Shoushan Zoo to see a variety of animals and enjoy a family-friendly attraction. Ticket prices may vary depending on current admission policies.\n\nKaohsiung offers plenty of ways to enjoy the city on different budgets. From free waterfront walks and lively night markets to cultural attractions and sightseeing activities, you can choose experiences based on your spending plan. Planning your [activities in Kaohsiung](https://www.airasia.com/blog/taiwan/kaohsiung/weekend-getaway-in-kaohsiung/) in advance and checking ticket prices online can help you make the most of your trip without overspending.\n\n## Food: Eat Well Without Overspending\n\nFood is one of the easiest parts of a Kaohsiung trip budget to adjust. From affordable Taiwanese street food and night-market snacks to casual restaurants and upscale dining, there are plenty of options for different budgets.\n\n**Typical food costs in Kaohsiung:**\n\n\\\\* **Street food & local stalls:** NT$40–100 per dish \\* **Casual restaurants:** NT$100–300 per meal \\* **Cafés & drinks:** NT$60–200 \\* **Night markets:** NT$100–300 for several snacks or a light meal \\* **Mid-range restaurants:** NT$300–700 per person \\* **Upscale dining:** NT$800+ per person\n\n**Must-try foods in Kaohsiung**\n\n1\\. **Beef noodles** – A comforting Taiwanese favourite made with tender beef, noodles and a rich broth. 2. **Beef rice noodles** – A local Kaohsiung speciality featuring rice noodles served with beef and flavourful broth. 3. **Seafood** – Enjoy fresh seafood dishes at local restaurants and markets, particularly around the harbour. 4. **Oyster omelette** – A popular Taiwanese street-food dish made with oysters, eggs and vegetables. 5. **Papaya milk** – A refreshing fruit drink commonly found at Taiwanese night markets. 6. **Stinky tofu** – A classic Taiwanese snack with a distinctive aroma, usually served fried with pickled vegetables and sauce. 7. **Taiwanese fried chicken** – Crispy chicken seasoned with Taiwanese spices and often served as a popular night-market snack. 8. **Pineapple cake** – A popular Taiwanese sweet that also makes a convenient souvenir.\n\nFor budget travellers, eating at **night markets, local eateries and food stalls** can help keep daily food costs low. If you want to experience more restaurants, cafés and seafood meals, allow a larger food budget.\n\nKaohsiung is particularly well known for its night-market food scene, so setting aside some of your daily budget for local snacks and drinks is a great way to experience the city without spending too much.\n\n## How Much to Bring to Kaohsiung: Budget Guidelines\n\nHere’s a realistic estimate for a 3–5 day trip in Kaohsiung\n\n| Category | Budget (NT$) | Mid-range (NT$) | Comfortable (NT$) |\n| --- | --- | --- | --- |\n| Transportation | NT$100–250 | NT$300–600 | NT$700–1,200 |\n| Food & Drinks | NT$300–600 | NT$700–1,200 | NT$1,500–2,500 |\n| Attractions | NT$0–300 | NT$400–800 | NT$1,000–2,000 |\n| **Total / Day** | **NT$400–1,150** | **NT$1,400–2,600** | **NT$3,200–5,700+** |\n\nDaily travel costs in Kaohsiung can vary depending on your travel style. Budget travellers can expect to spend around **NT$400–1,150 per day** by using public transport, eating at local restaurants and night markets, and visiting mostly free or low-cost attractions. A mid-range budget of **NT$1,400–2,600 per day** allows for more comfortable transport, restaurant meals, and several paid attractions. For a more comfortable experience with taxis, nicer dining, and premium activities, daily spending may reach **NT$3,200–5,700+**. Planning your expenses in advance helps you manage your budget while still enjoying the best of Kaohsiung. ✈\n\n| Category | Budget (NT$) | Mid-range (NT$) | Comfortable (NT$) |\n| --- | --- | --- | --- |\n| Transportation | NT$100–250 | NT$300–600 | NT$700–1,200 |\n| Food & Drinks | NT$300–600 | NT$700–1,200 | NT$1,500–2,500 |\n| Attractions | NT$0–300 | NT$400–800 | NT$1,000–2,000 |\n| **Total / Day** | **NT$400–1,150** | **NT$1,400–2,600** | **NT$3,200–5,700+** |\n\nDaily travel costs in Kaohsiung can vary depending on your travel style. Budget travellers can expect to spend around **NT$400–1,150 per day** by using public transport, eating at local restaurants and night markets, and visiting mostly free or low-cost attractions. A mid-range budget of **NT$1,400–2,600 per day** allows for more comfortable transport, restaurant meals, and several paid attractions. For a more comfortable experience with taxis, nicer dining, and premium activities, daily spending may reach **NT$3,200–5,700+**. Planning your expenses in advance helps you manage your budget while still enjoying the best of Kaohsiung. ✈\n\n## Budget-Friendly 5-Day Kaohsiung Itinerary with Estimated Costs\n\n![](https://www.airasia.com/cdn-cgi/image/width=1920,quality=75,format=auto/https://rad.airasia.com/assets/c03fb2d6-2f8d-4e2c-81ba-3ee8611075e1)\n\n**Day 1:** Explore Pier-2 Art Center, walk along the Love River, and enjoy the evening atmosphere at Liuhe Night Market.\n\n_Estimated Day Cost: NT$500–900 (~RM65–RM120)_\n\n**Day 2:** Visit Lotus Pond, explore the Dragon and Tiger Pagodas, and enjoy local food and shopping around Zuoying.\n\n_Estimated Day Cost: NT$400–800 (~RM55–RM110)_\n\n**Day 3:** Visit Fo Guang Shan Buddha Museum, explore its impressive grounds, and enjoy a relaxed evening back in Kaohsiung.\n\n_Estimated Day Cost: NT$500–1,000 (~RM70–RM140)_\n\n**Day 4:** Explore Cijin Island, enjoy the beach and seafood, then return to the city for an evening around the Kaohsiung waterfront.\n\n_Estimated Day Cost: NT$600–1,200 (~RM85–RM170)_\n\n**Day 5:** Explore local markets and shopping areas, pick up souvenirs, and enjoy a final Taiwanese meal before departure.\n\n_Estimated Day Cost: NT$400–800 (~RM55–RM110)_\n\n## How to Pay in Hong Kong\n\n![](https://www.airasia.com/cdn-cgi/image/width=1920,quality=75,format=auto/https://rad.airasia.com/assets/278d25fb-4b9f-45db-b8a3-5803eb575492)\n\n### Cash\n\nCash is still widely used for street food, markets, taxis, and small shops.\n\nCarry around THB 2,000–5,000 (~RM260–RM650) daily.\n\n### Digital Wallets\n\nMobile payment options such as Apple Pay, Google Pay and other contactless services may be accepted at participating businesses. Availability varies by merchant, so having a physical card or cash as a backup is recommended.\n\n### Credit / Debit Cards\n\nAccepted at hotels, malls, restaurants, and major attractions.\n\n### EasyCard and iPASS\n\nConvenient stored-value cards that can be used for public transport and at participating shops and businesses. They can make it easier to pay for everyday travel without carrying cash for every journey.\n\nTravelling to Kaohsiung is an exciting adventure filled with beautiful waterfront views, delicious Taiwanese food, vibrant night markets, shopping experiences, and unforgettable moments. By knowing how much to bring to Kaohsiung, planning transportation wisely, and using the right payment methods, you can explore the city comfortably without overspending.\n\nWith smart budgeting and planning, travelling to Kaohsiung in 2027 can be affordable, enjoyable, and truly unforgettable. Start planning your trip, pack your bags, and get ready to explore one of Taiwan’s most exciting cities.\n\n## Frequently Asked Questions (FAQ)\n\n### How much cash should I bring to Kaohsiung?\n\nA practical starting point is around NT$1,000–2,000 in cash per day, depending on your plans.\n\n### Is Kaohsiung expensive to visit?\n\nKaohsiung can be affordable, especially if you use public transport, eat local food and visit free attractions.\n\n### Can I use a credit card in Kaohsiung?\n\nYes. Cards are widely accepted at hotels, shopping malls, restaurants and larger businesses.\n\n### Should I bring cash or use a card in Kaohsiung?\n\nUse a combination of cash and cards. Cash is useful for markets and small vendors, while cards work well at larger businesses.\n\n### What currency should I use in Kaohsiung?\n\nThe official currency is the New Taiwan dollar (NT$), which is used for everyday purchases.\n\n### How to get cheap flights and hotels?\n\nAirAsia MOVE offers bundled deals for flights and hotels, often cheaper than booking separately.\n\n![Chayanit P.](https://www.airasia.com/cdn-cgi/image/width=1920,quality=75,format=auto/https://rad.airasia.com/assets/8c119dc7-896d-44c0-856e-6d3bc356692d)\n\n#### Chayanit P.\n\n4 September 2025",

        "summary": "The guide provides comprehensive information for travelers heading to Kaohsiung, Taiwan, detailing budgeting, payment options, accommodation, transportation, must-see attractions, and food costs. Key points include: \n1. **Budget Planning:** Travelers should budget around NT$1,000–2,000 in cash daily and set aside NT$500–1,000 as a buffer for unexpected expenses. A typical daily budget ranges from NT$400–5,700+, varying by travel style. \n2. **Accommodation:** Different lodging options are available from hostels for budget travelers to upscale hotels. Recommended areas include Formosa Boulevard and Yancheng District. \n3. **Transportation:** The MRT, public buses, and light rail are affordable ways to navigate the city, with connections to major attractions. \n4. **Attractions:** Kaohsiung features both free (e.g., Love River, Pier-2 Art Center) and paid attractions (e.g., Fo Guang Shan Buddha Museum). \n5. **Food Options:** The local food scene offers affordable to upscale dining, with street food averaging NT$40–300 per dish. Popular must-try foods include beef noodles and stinky tofu. \n6. **Tips for Savings:** Book flights in advance, consider bundled deals, and explore local markets to keep costs low."

        "url": "https://ymunt.org/transportation-guide",
        "title": "Transportation Guide - YMUNT",
        "description": "# Transportation Guide\n### **Traveling by MRT**\n- Single Journey: NT$20 - NT$65\n\n### **Taoyuan Airport MRT**\n**Fare Rates:** Single journey rides: NT$30 to NT$160.\n\n[Single-Journey Ticket Fares](https://www.tymetro.com.tw/tymetro-new/en/_pages/travel-guide/ticketson01.php)\n\n![](https://images.squarespace-cdn.com/content/v1/54444751e4b0c5dff2455cd5/ea9c284b-a74e-4da6-9abd-d70615f404d5/mrt_en.jpg)",
        "position": 13,
        "markdown": "Registration for YMUNT XIII is OPEN! Register early to secure a discount!\n\n# Transportation Guide\n\nThis page provides a list of potential transportation options and some other helpful tips when you are planning your travel around Taipei. If you have any questions, please feel free to contact [tw.delegations@ymunt.org](mailto:tw.delegations@ymunt.org?).\n\n### **Traveling by MRT**\n\nThe Taipei city metro hours of operation are 6 a.m. to midnight daily.\n\n**How to Pay and Where to Buy Passes**\n\n- Single Journey:Blue IC Single Journey Tokens can be purchased from the token vending machines and Metro station information counters in all stations.\n\n- One-day EasyCards Pass and 24-hour, 48-hour, 72-hour and All Pass: Available for purchase at all Metro station information counters. Each pass can only be used by one passenger at a time.\n\n\n**Fare Rates**\n\nAdmission to the trains is via blue plastic single-journey tokens or electronic stored value cards called [EasyCards](https://www.easycard.com.tw/en/service). Travelers can calculate single journey fare here, which depends on journey distance.\n\n- Single Journey: NT$20 - NT$65\n\n- One-day EasyCards Pass: NT$150\n\n- 24hr Taipei Metro Pass: NT$180\n\n- 48hr Taipei Metro Pass: NT$280\n\n- 72hr Taipei Metro Pass: NT$380\n\n- All Pass Ticket: NT$1,280 includes unlimited rides for 30 days on Taipei Metro, Taipei buses, and [YouBike](https://taipei.youbike.com.tw/) bike share.\n\n\nPassengers with EasyCards get a fare discount between subway and bus if the transfer is done within one hour.\n\n**Important Things to Know About Riding the MRT**\n\n- Eating, drinking, chewing gum, and smoking are not allowed.\n\n- Passengers must line up to board trains.\n\n- Don't sit in seats designated for the elderly, disabled, pregnant passengers, and those traveling with young children.\n\n- When riding the escalator, stand on the right and walk on the left.\n\n\n**Subway Lines**\n\n![](https://images.squarespace-cdn.com/content/v1/54444751e4b0c5dff2455cd5/87048698-0c02-458a-a505-959ce93d5e7d/routemap2024.png)\n\n### **Taoyuan Airport MRT**\n\nTaoyuan Airport MRT shortens the distance between Taipei and Taoyuan with the express service taking only 35 minutes from Taoyuan Airport Terminal 1 to Taipei Main Station and 38 minutes from Terminal 2 to Taipei Main Station.\n\n[_METRO ROUTE MAP_](https://www.tymetro.com.tw/tymetro-new/en/_pages/travel-guide/road.html \"METRO ROUTE MAP(open a new window)\")\n\n[_TIMETABLE_](https://www.tymetro.com.tw/tymetro-new/en/_pages/travel-guide/timetable.php \"TIMETABLE(open a new window)\")\n\n[_TRANSIT INFORMATION_](https://www.tymetro.com.tw/tymetro-new/en/_pages/travel-guide/transfer.html \"TRANSIT INFORMATION(open a new window)\")\n\n**Fare Rates:** Single journey rides: NT$30 to NT$160.\n\n**Express Train and Commuter Train**\n\nBefore boarding the MRT, please check if it is a purple express train or a blue commuter train. From Terminal 1, the express train takes 35 minutes to arrive at Taipei Main Station and the commuter train takes 50 minutes.\n\nFree 4G Wi-Fi is available at all stations. Express trains are equipped with wireless charging service and luggage racks.\n\n**Directions to Airport MRT Station**\n\nTerminal 1: The escalator south of B1 Food Court leads to A12 Airport Terminal 1 Station.\n\nTerminal 2: The pathway at the side of B2 Food Court leads to A13 Airport Terminal 2 Station.\n\n**Ticket and Service**\n\n[TICKET INFORMATION](https://www.tymetro.com.tw/tymetro-new/en/_pages/travel-guide/ticket.html \"TICKET INFORMATION(open a new window)\")\n\n[TAKING METHOD](https://www.tymetro.com.tw/tymetro-new/en/_pages/travel-guide/usage.html \"TAKING METHOD(open a new window)\")\n\n[Single-Journey Ticket Fares](https://www.tymetro.com.tw/tymetro-new/en/_pages/travel-guide/ticketson01.php \"Single-Journey Ticket Fares(open a new window)\")\n\n**Stations**\n\n![](https://images.squarespace-cdn.com/content/v1/54444751e4b0c5dff2455cd5/ea9c284b-a74e-4da6-9abd-d70615f404d5/mrt_en.jpg)\n\n### **Traveling by Bus**\n\nThe Taipei Joint Bus System includes general routes, trunk routes, MRT shuttles, mountain buses, recreational buses, and small buses. The bus fare of the Taipei Joint Bus System is based on sections (fare zones). For 2-or-more-section routes, the additional sectional fare is charged at place of alighting if the trip crosses the interaction point (or buffering zone). Most bus stops have electronic signs that countdown to when the next bus is arriving (passengers can also [track the bus](https://ebus.gov.taipei/Query/BusRoute)) and placards indicate the route and timetable.\n\n- [_Taipei eBus_](https://ebus.gov.taipei/Dybus.aspx \"Taipei eBus | Open Window\")\n\n- [_Real-time bus information_](https://ebus.gov.taipei/ebus \"Real-time bus information | Open Window\")\n\n- [_Trip planning_](https://ebus.gov.taipei/Transfer/TransPlan \"Trip planning | Open Window\")\n\n\n**Fare Rates:** The bus fare is charged based on sections, which impacts how much and how often you pay.\n\n- **One section:** NT$15\n\n- **Two sections:** NT$30\n\n- **Three sections:** NT$45\n\n\n**Payment:**\n\nBus fare is paid in cash or with contactless smart cards on board.\n\n1\\. Coin:\n\n- One-segment ticket, regular NT$15; Two-segment ticket, regular NT$30; Three-segment, NT$45. Most passengers will only need a One-segment ticket. The bus driver will notify you if you need to pay more for a Two or Three-segment ticket.\n\n- Note: Be sure to have the exact ticket amount as buses will not give change.\n\n\n2\\. Electronic payment:\n\n- Just lightly swipe your card over the sensor zone, and payment is automatically completed with the beeping of the reader.\n\n- Note: Passengers using smart cards have to swipe their cards upon both boarding and alighting from the bus. They will receive a transfer discount for the MRT, bus, and YouBike within one hour after alighting from the bus.\n\n\nDepending on the route, passengers either pay when getting on or off the bus or both. Look at the electronic sign above the driver:\n\n- If it has 上 **,** pay as you get on the bus.\n\n- If it has a 下, pay when you get off.\n\n- If you paid when you got on and the sign changes during your trip to 下, this indicates you have traveled through another zone and you have to pay again. Sometimes, the driver will hand you a paper ticket when you get on near where two zones meet. Keep this ticket and return it when you depart; it means you don’t have to pay a second time.\n\n\n**Important Things to Know:**\n\n- Double check the number and color of the bus before you get on. Some buses don’t follow the same route coming and going. The color indicated which subway line(s) the bus may stop at.\n\n- Stations are typically called out in Chinese or appear on a digital board. You can [plan your trip here](https://ebus.gov.taipei/Transfer/TransPlan) and [search for bus stops nearby](https://ebus.gov.taipei/Query/NearBus).\n\n\n![](https://images.squarespace-cdn.com/content/v1/54444751e4b0c5dff2455cd5/25d99bef-c43f-463a-bf64-39d0c03c14e7/original.jpg)\n\n### **Traveling by YouBike**\n\nTaipei bike sharing program [YouBike](https://taipei.youbike.com.tw/home) has more than 5,000 bicycles at 163 stations, Riders can use their MRT EasyCards, credit cards, or cellphones to rent one of the yellow and orange bicycles.\n\n- [Official Website](https://www.youbike.com.tw/region/main/)\n\n- [Station Locations](https://www.youbike.com.tw/region/main/stations/)\n\n\n**Fare Rates:**\n\n- $10NT per half-hour up to four hours.\n\n- $20NT per half-hour from four hours to eight hours.\n\n- $40NT per half-hour exceeding eight hours.\n\n\n![](https://images.squarespace-cdn.com/content/v1/54444751e4b0c5dff2455cd5/dc114f39-7d05-469b-b773-212d92038ad2/ubikestreet.jpg)\n\n­\n\n­\n\n# Registration is Open!\n\nEarly Registration for YMUNT XIII is now open! We encourage you to register as soon as possible to secure a seat for our conference. Find out more and register using the links below.\n\n- [REGISTER HERE](https://registration.ymunt.org/)",

        "summary": "Registration for YMUNT XIII is now open, encouraging early sign-ups for a discount. The page offers a comprehensive transportation guide for Taipei, highlighting options like the MRT, Taoyuan Airport MRT, buses, and YouBike. \n\n**MRT Details:**  \n- Operation hours: 6 a.m. to midnight.  \n- Payment: Single Journey Tokens and EasyCards available at stations.  \n- Fare Rates: NT$20 - NT$65 for single journey, passes available from NT$150 to NT$1,280 with discounts for transfers.  \n- Rules: No eating, drinking, or smoking; special seating rules; escalator etiquette.\n\n**Taoyuan Airport MRT:**  \n- Express service takes 35-38 mins to Taipei Main Station.  \n- Fares range from NT$30 to NT$160.  \n- Check for express or commuter trains when boarding.\n\n**Bus System:**  \n- Various routes and fare based on sections: NT$15 to NT$45.  \n- Payment options: cash or contactless smart cards, with transfer discounts available.\n\n**YouBike:**  \n- Bike-sharing program with over 5,000 bicycles.  \n- Fares: NT$10 for the first half-hour, increasing thereafter.  \n\nFor further details and to register for YMUNT XIII, visit the provided registration link."

        "url": "https://www.pelago.com/en-US/articles/taiwan-easycard-guide/",
        "title": "Taiwan EasyCard Guide (2026): Everything Travellers Need to Know",
        "description": "# Taiwan EasyCard Guide (2026): Everything Travellers Need to Know\n## 1. What is the Taiwan EasyCard?\nIt works in Taipei, Taoyuan, Taichung, Kaohsiung, and beyond.\n\n## 4. Where can you buy an EasyCard?\n### Taoyuan Airport\nMachines and counters generally run from 5am-11pm, but 7-Elevens remain open all night.\n\n## 5. Are there cute designs for the EasyCard?\n| Location | Availability | Notes |\n|-|-|-|\n| Taoyuan Airport | 5am-11pm | 24-hr convenience store backup |",
        "position": 14,
        "markdown": "[Home](https://www.pelago.com/en-US/)\n\n[BLOG](https://www.pelago.com/en-US/articles/)\n\nGet the app\n\n#### Get 10% off on the app with promo code APP10\n\nScan to download\n\nUSD\n\nCurrency\n\n- SGDSingapore Dollar\n- AUDAustralian Dollar\n- EUREuro\n- GBPPound Sterling\n- INRIndian Rupees\n- IDRIndonesian Rupiah\n- JPYJapanese Yen\n- HKDHong Kong Dollar\n- MYRMalaysian Ringgit\n- TWDNew Taiwan Dollar\n- THBThai Baht\n- PHPPhilippine Peso\n- USDU.S Dollar\n- NZDNew Zealand Dollar\n- VNDVietnamese Dong\n- KRWKorean Won\n- AEDEmirati Dirham\n- CNYChinese Yuan\n- CADCanadian Dollar\n- CHFSwiss Franc\n\nEnglish\n\nLanguage\n\n- 한국어\n- 日本語\n- English\n- Bahasa Indonesia\n- Tiếng Việt\n- ไทย\n- 简体中文\n\nSupport\n\n- Virtual assistant\n- [Help center](https://www.pelago.com/helpcenter/en/)\n- Share your feedback\n\nCart\n\nLog in\n\nSign up\n\nSign up to enjoy 10% off your first 2 bookings\n\n[Taipei](https://www.pelago.com/en-US/articles/city/taipei/) [Taiwan](https://www.pelago.com/en-US/articles/country/taiwan/)\n\nShare this article\n\n# Taiwan EasyCard Guide (2026): Everything Travellers Need to Know\n\nAlexandria Lopez\n\nLast updated: Mar 4, 20266 minutes\n\n![A collage titled “Taiwan EasyCard Guide” featuring EasyCards, Taipei skyline, a metro train, and a hand holding an illustrated card.](https://images.ctfassets.net/dsbipkqphva2/6kLlRdBBPISmteM7FfYUr9/26183e34f98ab3a0fd58ef74b4e2960a/taiwan-easycard-guide-pelago.png?fm=webp&w=1192)\n\nPlanning a trip to Taiwan?\n\nTime to sort out your travel game. Smooth metro rides that glide like silk. Convenience store snacks at the tap of a card. Bike rentals that just work.\n\n[The Taiwan EasyCard](https://www.pelago.com/en-US/activity/pnz7h-taiwan-easycard--collect-from-airport-taiwan/) is your golden ticket to tap-and-go freedom across this incredible island.\n\nHere's your 2026-friendly guide to navigating Taiwan like you've lived there forever, thanks to the Taiwan EasyCard.\n\n## 1\\. What is the Taiwan EasyCard?\n\n_Photo: @iamjoyceuy on Instagram_\n\nEasyCard is Taiwan's most popular reloadable smartcard for public transportation and other purchases.\n\nOne tap. That's all it takes. MRTs, city buses, TRA local trains, YouBikes – even that late-night 7-Eleven run for bubble tea. The EasyCard handles it all without breaking a sweat.\n\nThink of it as Taiwan's most popular sidekick. Unlike those tourist-only passes, locals use EasyCards every single day. It works in Taipei, Taoyuan, Taichung, Kaohsiung, and beyond. Taipei MRT users get a sweet monthly rebate when they use an EasyCard.\n\nThis little card also opens doors to thousands of cashless spots – supermarkets, pharmacies, parking meters, even select attractions and restaurants. It's like having a magic wand for seamless payments, plus those lovely fare discounts that add up fast.\n\n## 2\\. How much does the EasyCard Cost?\n\n_Photo: @\\_nakamayumi on Instagram_\n\n- **Card cost:** USD 3 (non-refundable)\n\n- **No preloaded value:** Top up with cash after purchase\n\n- **Recommended top-up:** USD 13-16 for short trips\n\n- **Maximum stored value:** You can load up to USD 958 on the card, which is more than enough even for longer visits.\n\n\n## 3\\. Does the EasyCard have an expiration date?\n\n_Photo: @cocito77 on Instagram_\n\nHere's some beautiful news: your EasyCard never expires.\n\nInactive cards for over 2 years may need a simple top-up to reactivate. This means you can bring the card back on your next trip without issue.\n\nIf you forget to top up or don't have enough balance, some transport systems allow your EasyCard to go into a negative balance (up to USD 2) so you can finish your journey. Just remember to top it up before your next ride.\n\n## 4\\. Where can you buy an EasyCard?\n\n### MRT Stations\n\nEvery MRT station in Taipei, Taoyuan, and Kaohsiung sells EasyCards at service counters or vending machines. Machines operate during MRT hours (from 6am-12mn) and provide bilingual instructions. You can purchase an EasyCard at any MRT station in Taiwan.\n\n### Convenience Stores\n\nMajor chains like 7-Eleven, FamilyMart, Hi-Life, and OK Mart sell EasyCards at the cashier. Many are open 24/7, making this the most flexible option. The staff can also help with card top-ups and balance checks.\n\n### Taoyuan Airport\n\nPick one up at the Airport MRT station, convenience stores, or tourist counters. Machines and counters generally run from 5am-11pm, but 7-Elevens remain open all night. It's great for late-night arrivals.\n\n### Online on Pelago\n\nWant to skip the airport hunt entirely?\n\n[Pelago lets you snag your EasyCard](https://www.pelago.com/en-US/activity/pnz7h-taiwan-easycard--collect-from-airport-taiwan/) before you even board your flight. Tourist-friendly packages that you can book ahead of time and collect at Taoyuan International Airport the second you arrive.\n\nNo stress about sold-out stations or stores. No hunting for cute designs that vanished overnight. Plus, you can grab preloaded credit so you're ready to roll from minute one. Plus, your purchase will let you [earn KrisFlyer miles](https://www.pelago.com/en-US/articles/how-to-earn-krisflyer-miles/), which is perfect for your next destination trip.\n\nAnd here's the cherry on top: Pelago bundles deals like [pairing your EasyCard with a Taiwan SIM Card](https://www.pelago.com/en-US/activity/pfecsw05q-taiwan-sim-card-pickup-at-taoyuan-airport-or-airport-mrt-4g-5g-unlimited-data-easycard-ipass-chunghwa-telecom-pre-paid-sim-card-register-real-name-to-get-1-extra-day-for-free-taipei/). Everything sorted in one go.\n\n## 5\\. Are there cute designs for the EasyCard?\n\n_Photo: @ochibawolf on Instagram_\n\nLooking for a cute EasyCard Taiwan design?\n\nLimited editions (Hello Kitty, anime characters, local mascots) are sold at convenience stores, museums, and pop-ups. Expect to pay USD 6-12 depending on the design. Some EasyCards feature popular characters like Hello Kitty and Pokémon, appealing to collectors and fans. Others even have unique designs, such as shaped as a Harry Potter wand or as a plushie.\n\n| Location | Availability | Notes |\n| --- | --- | --- |\n| MRT stations | 6am-12mn | Counter and vending machine |\n| Convenience stores | 24/7 | Good for cute designs |\n| Taoyuan Airport | 5am-11pm | 24-hr convenience store backup |\n| Online (Pelago) | Pre-order anytime | Pick up at airport |\n\n**Note:** If you buy a limited-edition EasyCard, this premium is non-refundable and not counted as stored value. Limited edition EasyCards, such as those commemorating anniversaries or featuring popular themes, are often highly sought after and can sell out quickly.\n\n## 6\\. How do I use an EasyCard?\n\n### MRT & Metro\n\nTap in and out at gates. Fare is auto-deducted based on distance. Gates show the remaining balance.\n\n### Buses\n\nTap on when boarding and off when alighting. Signs or driver announcements indicate whether you tap once or twice.\n\n### TRA Local Trains\n\nUse on non-reserved local trains under 70km. Tap at entry and exit gates. It’s ideal for spontaneous day trips.\n\n### YouBike\n\n_Photo: @benbenben365 on Instagram_\n\nRegister your card with a Taiwan mobile number via the YouBike app or kiosk. Alternatively, use the YouBike 2.0 app with a credit card. Once linked, just tap your card on the bike dock. The EasyCard is also accepted for other shared bicycle rentals T-Bike and C-Bike across various cities.\n\n### Stores and Services\n\nEasyCard is accepted at most convenience stores, vending machines, parking meters, some cafes, bakeries, and even at select tourist attractions like the [Taipei Zoo](https://www.pelago.com/en-US/activity/pqfe6-taipei-zoo-taipei/) and [Maokong Gondola](https://www.pelago.com/en-US/activity/pjfrodmn7-taipei-maokong-gondola-ticket-taipei/). Using an EasyCard for MRT and bus rides gives you around a 20% discount compared to cash payments.\n\n[Taipei\\\\\n\\\\\n**Taipei Zoo Tickets**\\\\\n\\\\\nFrom\\\\\n\\\\\nUSD 3.18\\\\\n\\\\\n4.5\\\\\n\\\\\nBook now](https://www.pelago.com/en-US/activity/pqfe6-taipei-zoo-taipei/)\n\n## 7\\. Where can I use the EasyCard?\n\n_Photo: @taiwannearme.official on Instagram_\n\n- Taipei MRT\n\n- Taoyuan Airport MRT\n\n- Taipei city buses\n\n- TRA (Taiwan Railway) short-distance trains\n\n- YouBike rentals\n\n- Convenience stores\n\n- Some cafes, parking meters, vending machines\n\n- Limited attractions and ferries\n\n- All metro lines in Taipei, Taichung, and Kaohsiung, as well as local trains on Taiwan Railways\n\n\nIt is not accepted on the High-Speed Rail (HSR) or most long-distance buses. For HSR, you must purchase separate tickets in advance or at the station.\n\n## 8\\. How do I top-up the EasyCard?\n\n_Photo: @jhsnaps.jpg on Instagram_\n\nTop-ups are almost always cash-only, but there you can also use mobile wallets if you have a Taiwan SIM card.\n\n1. **MRT Stations**: Use add-value machines. Insert NTD 100 (USD 3), NTD 500 (USD 16), or NTD 1,000 (USD 32) notes.\n\n2. **Convenience Stores:** Ask the cashier. A popular choice if you're also buying snacks or water.\n\n3. **Mobile App (EasyWallet)**: Mostly useful for locals. You’ll need a Taiwan SIM card and local ID to unlock full features like auto top-up or balance tracking. If you do have a local number, the app lets you view past transactions and card balance.\n\n4. **Credit Card?** Not accepted for top-ups. You can only use cards for pre-purchase platforms like Pelago.\n\n\n**Negative Balance Feature:** Your card can go up to USD 2 if you don't have enough credit for a ride. You must top-up before using it again.\n\n## 9\\. How can I check my EasyCard balance?\n\nYou can view recent trip history and top-up transactions at MRT station counters or using the EasyWallet app (with Taiwan SIM). You can also check your balance on the EasyCard website, but it's only available in Chinese.\n\nAlternatively, [you can use the EasyWallet app](https://play.google.com/store/apps/details?id=com.easycard.wallet&hl=en) on your Android NFC-enabled phones or on other third-party apps. Apple devices currently do not support this.\n\n## 10\\. Can I refund my EasyCard?\n\n_Photo: @taiwannearme.official on Instagram_\n\nThe card fee is non-refundable. However, you can refund the money you topped-up onto your card.\n\n- Card fee (USD 3): Non-refundable\n\n- Stored value: Refundable minus a USD 0.64 processing fee\n\n\nRefunds available at:\n\n- Any Taipei MRT service counter\n\n- Taoyuan Airport MRT station (ideal before departure)\n\n\nIf your balance is under USD 0.64, it's best to spend it at a convenience store rather than refund it.\n\n## 11\\. What's the difference between the EasyCard and the iPass?\n\n_Photo: @cocito77 on Instagram_\n\nEasyCard and iPass are the two most common transportation smartcards in Taiwan. Here's what you can expect from both of them.\n\n| **Feature** | **EasyCard** | **iPASS** |\n| --- | --- | --- |\n| **Origin** | Taipei | Kaohsiung |\n| **Usage Area** | Nationwide | Nationwide |\n| **Mobile Apps** | EasyWallet | iPASS Money |\n| **Maximum Balance** | USD 958 | USD 320 |\n| **Bicycle Rental** | Yes (can be used on YouBike and more) | No (cannot be used on YouBike) |\n| **Tourist Use** | Excellent | Also excellent |\n\nFunctionally, they're nearly the same. EasyCard is more common in northern cities, while iPASS is prevalent in the south. Both are used nationwide and accepted across most transit systems and retail chains.\n\n## 12\\. Can I use mobile wallets to store my EasyCard?\n\n- **Apple Pay / Apple Wallet:** Not supported for EasyCard\n\n- **Samsung Pay:** Only works with Taiwan-issued Samsung phones\n\n- **EasyWallet App:** Useful for locals with a Taiwan phone number and ID. Allows limited functions like transaction history and balance check\n\n\nSome newer MRT gates accept contactless Visa/Mastercard/Apple Pay, but it is not yet available system-wide. Until fully rolled out, carry and use a physical EasyCard.\n\n## 13\\. Is the EasyCard worth it for tourist?\n\nAbsolutely. For USD 3+ top-ups, EasyCard provides:\n\n- Seamless travel across MRTs (Only valid on non-reserved, local trains. For express or reserved-seat TRA trains, you’ll need a separate ticket.)\n\n- Can also be used on buses and YouBikes\n\n- Small transfer discounts (e.g. USD 0.25 off when transferring between MRT and buses within 60 minutes)\n\n- Quick purchases at stores\n\n- Avoids carrying coins or queuing for tickets\n\n- No expiry (use again on future visits)\n\n- Works in multiple cities with one card\n\n\nUnless you're taking only taxis or private tours, the card pays for itself in convenience.\n\n## 14\\. Can I share my EasyCard?\n\n_Photo: @abbinbin on Instagram_\n\nNo. One card per person. This is because the card is used for proper entry and exit tracking.\n\n## 15\\. What happens if I lose my EasyCard?\n\n_Photo: @selection\\_ace on Instagram_\n\nUnregistered cards cannot be recovered. You'll need to buy a new one.\n\n## 16\\. Do children need an EasyCard?\n\n_Photo: @kanahei\\_ting on Instagram_\n\nYes, children aged 6 and above need their own EasyCard to use public transport. Children under 6 (or under 115cm in height) may travel for free on some systems when accompanied by a fare-paying adult.\n\nThere are concession EasyCards available for students and children, which offer discounted fares. However, these require proof of age or student ID and are typically issued to Taiwan residents. Tourists should purchase the standard adult EasyCard for children unless they have local documentation.\n\n## 17\\. Are there any more tips before I buy my EasyCard?\n\n_Photo: @missc\\_simplelife on Instagram_\n\n- Top up at least USD 15-16 to start your trip smoothly\n\n- Keep NTD 100 notes (around USD 3) handy for machines\n\n- Use your card for small purchases to reduce coin clutter\n\n- Tap cleanly (not with a wallet full of other cards)\n\n- Download the EasyWallet app to track usage (only if you have an Android phone and Taiwan SIM; not functional with international numbers)\n\n- Use the remaining balance before flying out; buy snacks or souvenirs\n\n- [Consider Pelago for EasyCard + SIM card](https://www.pelago.com/en-US/activity/pfecsw05q-taiwan-sim-card-pickup-at-taoyuan-airport-or-airport-mrt-4g-5g-unlimited-data-easycard-ipass-chunghwa-telecom-pre-paid-sim-card-register-real-name-to-get-1-extra-day-for-free-taipei/) or transport bundles\n\n- Hang on to your card for future visits; it never expires\n\n\n## Ready to Explore Taiwan?\n\nThe EasyCard Taiwan isn't just a transit tool.\n\nIt's your stress-free passport to exploring this incredible island. It simplifies everything, saves time from day one, and lets you wander with complete confidence.\n\nGrab one at the airport or station and discover Taiwan like you were meant to.\n\n##### Share this article\n\nWritten by Alexandria Lopez\n\n_Alexandria has been writing ever since she was 17 years old. This paired with her love of travel turns wanderlust into words.         "summary": "The article provides a comprehensive guide on the Taiwan EasyCard, a versatile smartcard for easy travel and payments across Taiwan. Key points include: \n1. **What is the EasyCard**: A reloadable card used for public transport, stores, and attractions.\n2. **Cost**: The card itself costs USD 3 and requires additional top-ups (recommended USD 13-16).\n3. **Expiration**: EasyCards do not expire; however, inactive cards may need a top-up to reactivate.\n4. **Purchase Locations**: Available at MRT stations, convenience stores, and online through Pelago.\n5. **Design Options**: Limited edition designs are collectible, sold at various locations.\n6. **Usage**: Cards are used for entering/exiting transport and for cashless purchases at many establishments.\n7. **Top-Up Process**: Mostly cash-based but can be done via local mobile wallets.\n8. **Refunds**: The card fee is non-refundable; however, the stored balance can be refunded with a processing fee.\n9. **Comparison to iPass**: The EasyCard is more commonly accepted in northern Taiwan, compared to the iPass used more in southern regions.\n10. **Tourist Benefits**: Great for tourists as it simplifies transport, offers fare discounts, and never expires.\nOverall, the EasyCard is highly recommended for efficient travel and payment solutions in Taiwan."
      },

        "markdown": "TOP [Go to the main content section](https://www.travel.taipei/en/information/bus#main-content \"Go to the main content section\")\n\n::: [Home](https://www.travel.taipei/en \"Home\") [Information](https://www.travel.taipei/en/information/trafficlist \"Information\") [Transportation Information](https://www.travel.taipei/en/information/trafficlist \"Transportation Information\") Public Bus\n\n## ![](https://www.travel.taipei/Content/images/content/transport/09.jpg) Public Bus\n\nAnchor point\n\n- [Share to Facebook](https://www.facebook.com/sharer/sharer.php?u=https%3a%2f%2fwww.travel.taipei%2fen%2finformation%2fbus \"Share to Facebook|Open Window\")\n- [Post on X!](https://twitter.com/home/?status=Public+Bus%20https%3a%2f%2fwww.travel.taipei%2fen%2finformation%2fbus \"Post on X!|Open Window\")\n- [Share to Weibo](http://service.weibo.com/share/share.php?url=https%3a%2f%2fwww.travel.taipei%2fen%2finformation%2fbus&title=Public+Bus&language=en \"Share to Weibo|Open Window\")\n- [LINE it!](http://line.naver.jp/R/msg/text/?Public+Bus%20https%3a%2f%2fwww.travel.taipei%2fen%2finformation%2fbus \"LINE it!|Open Window\")\n\n![Bus](https://www.travel.taipei/Content/images/static/information/trafficlist06.jpg)\n\nThe Taipei Joint Bus System includes general routes, trunk routes, MRT shuttles, mountain buses, recreational buses, and small buses. It is your best option to explore Taipei City.\n\nThe bus fare of the Taipei Joint Bus System is based on sections (fare zones). For 2-or-more-section routes, the additional sectional fare is charged at place of alighting if the trip crosses the interaction point (or buffering zone).\n\n- [Taipei eBus](https://ebus.gov.taipei/Dybus.aspx \"Taipei eBus | Open Window\")\n- [Real-time bus information](https://ebus.gov.taipei/EBus2/RouteList2 \"Real-time bus information | Open Window\")\n- [Trip planning](https://ebus.gov.taipei/Transfer/TransPlan \"Trip planning | Open Window\")\n- [_Taipei Sightseeing Bus_](https://www.taipeisightseeing.com.tw/en \"Taipei Sightseeing Bus & Taipei Resturant Bus  | Open Window\")\n\n### Tickets\n\nBus fare is paid in cash or with contactless smart cards on board.\n\n- #### 1.Coin:\n\nOne-segment ticket, regular NT$15; Two-segment ticket, regular NT$30; Three-segment, NT$45. Most passengers will only need a One-segment ticket. The bus driver will notify you if you need to pay more for a Two or Three-segment ticket.\n\n**※Note: Be sure to have the exact ticket amount as buses will not give change.**\n- #### 2.Electronic payment:\n\nJust lightly swipe your card over the sensor zone, and payment is automatically completed with the beeping of the reader.\n\n**※Note: Passengers using smart cards have to swipe their cards upon both boarding and alighting from the bus.         "summary": "The Taipei Joint Bus System provides comprehensive public transportation options, including general, trunk, MRT shuttles, mountain, recreational, and small buses, making it ideal for exploring the city.
